# Path register

One row per topic. A row exists before its article does. No row, no trace; no trace, no article.

Status: `written` the article exists · `reviewed` a reviewer re-derived it from source, at the version its metadata names. A row carrying any other word is refused.

## A reviewer finding declined, with its reason

The verify stage twice asked for the `Created … Last modified …` footer to be
removed from the articles under review, as apparatus outside the front matter.
It is declined, and the same answer holds for any article: the footer is the
convention on all of them, the check knows it by name and excludes it from the
word count, and removing it from three would make those three the odd ones out
rather than fix anything. Changing it is a wiki-wide decision, not a finding
against an article. The reviewer withdrew it once on exactly that ground before
raising it again.

## A count that renumbers

Three verify rounds running moved the same number. An article said three
endings leave a position alone, then four, then five, each round turning up a
branch the last had missed, and the sentence after it read "a fourth goes the
other way" — an ordinal whose meaning came from the count before it. The sixth
round found two more, which are the ones that turn an outcome back before any
handling at all.

The count was the writer's grouping, not the code's. Where the code enumerates —
six stages in the walk — a number is a fact with a source. Where the writer
groups branches, it is an arrangement that the next reading undoes. The gate
refuses an ordinal standing in for a thing, since that is the form whose
meaning lives in a count nothing holds; an ordinal qualifying a noun says what
it counts and is left alone.

A tally of part of a group was refused too, at first. Running that across every
article showed what the form does not separate: "nine of the ten are logged as a
warning" is a fact with a source, and "only two of them read the order" was the
writer's grouping. The gate cannot tell those apart, and refusing both would
have meant rewriting a dozen correct sentences, so that half was withdrawn after
one pass over the corpus.

What survives is the definite ordinal with no anchor. One after a *first* in
the same section is left alone — the reader has the list in front of them — and
one whose first is in another section, or nowhere, is refused.

The indefinite form goes unread. *A third* is a fraction, *a second* is usually
a length of time, and *a fourth* is as often another one as the one numbered
four, none of which a pattern separates from the defect. The same judgement
settled the purpose check, which stops at any word opening
another clause and so lets a compound subject past: a check that blocks
correct prose costs more than one that misses, every time.

*that* is the exception, and it is deliberate. The defect the check exists for
reads "because a venue that cannot be reached must not leave the host unable to
stop", so a relative clause sits inside the real case and cannot open an
exemption. An obligation inside a noun phrase is therefore refused,
whether the clause is marked — "a response that the caller must acknowledge" —
or not — "the size the position must release has moved". The writer rewords
them. That is the one known false positive, kept because dropping it would
cost the catch.

## A purpose the code is not under

Four verify rounds running turned up the same defect in different articles: a
true fact with a reason attached that the code does not hold. The bypass skipped
five stages for an exit, and the article said those were the conditions an urgent
exit most needs to get out under — the bypass tests no urgency. The shutdown
passed over a failed send, and the article said a venue that cannot be reached
must not leave the host unable to stop — the code catches every exception and
names no venue.

The signal both share is a purpose clause carrying an obligation: *because*,
*so that*, *lest* or *on the grounds that*, and then *must*, *should*,
*ought*, *need to*, *needs to*, *has to*, *have to* or *cannot be
allowed*. Code holds conditions, so a reason given as something the system
is obliged to do is the writer explaining rather than reporting. The
gate refuses that pairing and leaves a bare cause alone, a cause being a fact
about the path: an order the
venue rejects is recorded as a success because the venue answered.

## What a row's state means

A row is `written` or `reviewed`. Reviewed means a reviewer re-derived the article
from the source its claims cite, and whatever that turned up was settled — fixed,
or declined with a reason on this page. Reading it and agreeing it sounds right is
not that, and neither is a review that found things nobody acted on.

Review is a fact about a version, not something a page keeps. A row reading
`reviewed` names the version it covers in the article's own metadata, and the
check refuses it where that stamp is absent, older than the article's last
change, or later than it — a review cannot cover a version that never existed.
Editing a reviewed article therefore un-reviews it, with no judgement needed
from anyone.

The check also refuses any other word in that column, and a count that disagrees
with the table, so the unreviewed rows are a number on this page rather than an
absence nobody sees.

**The count below read zero for a long time because the rule was wrong.** It was
set to mean a verify pass that returned nothing at all, which an asked reviewer
almost never does: the last pass over three articles returned thirty-two findings
of `supported` against a handful of corrections, and still read as not reviewed.
A review that finds things, has them fixed, and is confirmed against the fixed
text is a review. One that finds things nobody acts on is not.

**6 of them reviewed, 313 not.**

Package 1 leans on terms it never defines. Across its thirteen bodies a reader meets venue, account, fill, run, ladder and slice, and the two price articles rest on market order and order book throughout. A reader meeting any of these for the first time has nowhere to go.

Numbering starts at P2-11 because the harvest already bound P2-01 to P2-04 to modification, the bracket change, run cancellation and the schedule change. Those rows now name their subject rather than an ID that pointed at nothing.

Order follows dependency. A venue and an account own nothing else and are used most. The book and the order types come next because the two price articles rest on both without defining either. A fill is one execution at the venue; holdings are what a fill changes, netting a fee the command's own total does not. The spine is what package 1 calls writing down and recording, which currently stand unexplained. Outcomes own *partial*, which is a report status and not a kind of fill — that distinction is why a fill and an outcome are two rows and not one. Runs own *slice*; ladders are a separate arm of the dispatch that submits every rung at once, so neither folds into the other. A hidden-size order is a third arm again, sending one order for the whole amount while showing the venue a smaller one. Deadlines are seeded rather than held back because there are two clocks, not one: unstarted work is judged from when it was accepted, and a run that has started is judged from a fresh window opened when it started.

Two costs were known before tracing started. Each article makes every unlinked mention of its term in package 1 a check failure, so each carries a pass back through the existing ten. And the term check only carries terms whose string cannot mean anything else. The rest were put to a reviewer against all ten bodies, phrase by phrase, before any of these articles was written. The mentions that must *not* link:

| Phrase | In | Why not |
|---|---|---|
| "until the order is filled", "too thin to fill" | estimating the likely price | the book walk completing, not a recorded execution |
| "a held or failed account", "on hold", "what holds a pass back" | the worker, the priority line, work already under way | an account suspended, not inventory |
| "the run stays on the books" | work already under way | an idiom; neither the order book nor a spine write |
| "this runs early", "cancellations still run", "until the money runs out" | work already under way, the priority line, estimating | execution order and exhaustion, not a fed-out run |
| "when a send's outcome is unknown" | its own title, and two links to it | whether the send arrived, not the request's outcome |
| "refuse the order on the result", "stopped by the result" | the likely-price check | the calculation's result |
| "the worker logs it", "the record is put back for a later pass" | a reply that cannot be read, work already under way | diagnostic logging and retry state, not spine writes |

Two facts fall out of that pass. The spine's subject is carried entirely without its name — "writes down", "records", "the intent stays recorded" across five articles — so a string check would have found none of it. And *partial* appears in no package-1 body at all, so the second half of P2-18 attaches to nothing there.


Two of the rows above were carved out while writing their parent: the expiry cancel from deadlines, and the pre-queue gates from order placement, both because stating their branches accurately took the parent past the cap.

### Held back from package 2

| Topic | Why |
|---|---|
| What an execution mode is | A survey of the modes is a table of contents until the modes themselves are written. The bodies never use the word. |
| What a linked pair is | The thirteen bodies never use it; the mentions are in evidence only. It reads as venue machinery, so it follows P2-11 if it is wanted at all. |
| What a slice is | Owned by P2-19 rather than standing alone. |

Two foundations and two of the subjects the brief names. Cancellation is written, which unblocked modification; modification was put behind the vocabulary rather than ahead of it, since it is carried out as a cancel followed by a replacement and both ends are terms package 2 defines. It was written later, as P3-13.

Nine of the thirteen articles exist because another outgrew the limit. P1-11 was carved out when review found the wait described as the gap between passes: the wait is reached only where a pass has nothing to take, and a pass that takes a request goes straight round again. Stating that took the parent to 348 words, and what a pass does for an account that is reconciling or poisoned is a subject of its own. P2-46 was carved out of P2-22 when review found the venue's own sizes weighed on the way out only where they had been loaded for the symbol, which is a subject three articles were each stating a corner of. P1-12 was carved out of P1-11 in turn when review found a deferred change put back on the line every pass for as long as the hold lasts, a failed hold never lifting: stating that took the parent past the cap, and the ways a worker stops taking work are a subject rather than a list of exceptions to one. P1-13 was carved out of P1-11 after that, when review corrected the hold lifecycle twice over — a reconciling hold lifts whenever a later pass finishes, a poisoned one never does, and a change taken off the line by a cancelled pass is gone — which is a subject of its own rather than a paragraph inside what a held pass does. P1-10 was carved out when review found that an unreadable venue reply has four different endings depending on where the reading fails, and stating them took the parent past the cap. P1-09 was carved out when review found a whole stage missing from the worker article — a requested sweep of seven repair jobs, the first of which repeats the cancellation retry with its timing test dropped. Stating that accurately took the parent past the cap. P1-07 and P1-08 were carved out when the word count was corrected to include heading text: converting run-in bold paragraphs into headings during the voice rewrite had moved words off the counted side, and fixing that put four articles over. P1-07 was anticipated below as "an unclear answer from the venue"; P1-08 had already been linked by name from another article, which is the sign a term deserves its own page. P1-05 was carved out of P1-02 when the book check grew into a subject of its own. P1-06 was then carved out of P1-05 when a review added the sell-side sign flip and the ways the book can yield nothing, pushing it to 332 words: the check itself stayed in P1-05, the arithmetic moved to P1-06. P4-25 was split from P4-03 before writing, as P4-22 was from P4-02: the refusals are a subject and the settings are another. P4-26 was then carved out of P4-25 when review found the refusal list half the size it should be — six deployment caps, the epoch, the shutdown limit, the endpoint port and an unparseable simulator address were all missing, and a secrets file that will not decode escapes the catch meant for credentials. The environment's refusals and an account's are two lists, which is where the cut fell. P4-33 was split from P4-07 when review probed each check against the pinned bodies and found three it lets through: a truthy value of any kind on the four name fields, a timeout that is not a number or is endless, and an endless reference price. Each is recorded as a harvest row. P4-32 was then carved out when review found the replacement rule conditional: a difference already remembered makes no fresh report, so a shortfall that deepened and eased back leaves the deeper report waiting past the point the books show it. P4-31 was split from P4-06 before writing: what crosses is one subject, and when a shortfall counts as one — a tolerance per asset, a surplus passed over, the same difference suppressed, and a report kept for retry — is another. P4-30 was carved out of P4-05 when review found the forgetting conditional in a way the article had flattened: nothing is applied for a duplicate only where its own name went through before, and an ending can carry a name the far end has never seen. P4-29 was split from P4-05 before writing, for the same reason: how many outcomes one crossing makes is a subject, and what a fill carries back is another. P4-28 was split from P4-04 before writing: the crossing re-keys five fields and rebuilds one payload, and the payload has three shapes, two refusals and one mode that behaves unlike the rest. P4-27 was then carved out in turn when the caps needed four shapes stating at once — a positive finite decimal for four of them, a positive whole number for the other two, an empty setting counting as unset, and unset refused only on live — taking the parent to 353 words. P4-24 was carved out when review found two more branches in how an abandonment lands — a child the venue refuses stays active while the rest are still attempted, and a ladder caught mid-change gives up at the first child it fails to cancel — taking the parent to 321 words. Six ways one abandonment can land is a subject, not a paragraph. P4-23 was carved out when review found stopping described as if each step were insulated: a cancel the venue refuses, a drain that runs out of time and a close that fails are each logged and passed over, but one unregister failure raises, leaves trading marked as started, and carries out of the host with its connection and loop unclosed. P4-22 was split from P4-02 before writing rather than after: the row named two subjects, the host's six stopping steps and the fate of open work, and the second runs to two passes, three ways an abandonment can land, one order deliberately left standing and a wait that gives up. P4-21 was carved out of P4-20 when review separated trading raising while it starts from trading merely taking too long, pushing it to 315 words. What a host says about itself while starting was never a kind of failure, so that is the half that moved. P4-20 was carved out of P4-01 when review found the startup order understated in three places at once — three of the seven calls can return having done nothing, the key-check timeout stops nothing, and one account failing only asks the others down — and stating all three took the parent to 363 words. P3-24 was carved out of P3-04 when review corrected where each reported figure comes from: the counts are read off the record, the summary is built from the record and the scenario together, and the profit is read from neither, pushing the parent to 321 words. Both splits were forced by the word count, which is the mechanism working. Expect the same for a venue refusing an order, and for an unclear answer from the venue.

## Package 4 — the host, the seams, and the leaves

Thirty-three Praxis modules were named nowhere in [the ledger](ledger.md) when this package was seeded. The largest was `launcher.py`, which every package until then had cited and none had traced. The rest fall into three groups: the seams between Nexus and Praxis, the shapes a request is carried in, and the leaves the replay and metrics rows stopped short of.

Order follows dependency again. The host comes first because the settings it reads decide what every other subject is allowed to do, and because four written articles already cite it without a page to point at. The seams come next: a decision crossing into Praxis and an outcome crossing back are one subject each, and reconciliation is a third because it speaks a different vocabulary in each direction. The shapes follow the seams, since a command is what a crossing produces. The leaves come last; they are reached from pages that already exist.

Nexus is in scope. All 70 of its modules are named, none untouched. 64 carry a row of their own, and 6 are reached by an article, a recorded finding, or a recorded exclusion: all six recorded as shapes with no behaviour to trace. None is `cited, not traced` any longer.

## Package 5 — the Praxis half of the seam

Scope: what lets go of a command's unwritten mark and how far that letting go
reaches, what a failed settled-record write costs the next start, which starts
square their figures against nothing at all, the road an arriving report takes
and what it reads on the way, and the window in which the store writes without
the locks it is later handed. The five candidates the package-9 re-seed named,
taken through the stages together. This is the fifth package on the Praxis side;
the Nexus run reached nine and closed that side's log, and what it left open is
mostly this seam.

What lets go of the mark is the subject that must come before the rest. N8-03
has that mark standing where an earlier result for the same command carried one
of the other two, and no article says what drops it, so a reader of N8-03 is
left taking the mark for something each command clears on its own account. It is
let go in bulk, and by a write that went down: such a write drops the mark of
every command the copy taken before it holds, unless a fresher mark for that
command has landed by the time the copy is walked. The command whose outcome
asked for the write need not be in that copy, and a write that raises drops
nothing.

### Merge and promote, the Praxis half of the seam

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-289 | Each unwritten mark carries a number one higher than the last. | Merge with H-292: the number is what the drop weighs, so the two are one fact read from either end |
| Row H-290 | A later result for a marked command that succeeded asks for a write even where it reports nothing changed. | Decline. N8-03 states it, as the third of the three marks that ask for a write |
| Row H-291 | The write puts down the whole state, the positions and the capital figures among it. | Decline. N8-03 states it in its first sentence, and N7-08 lists what a checkpoint holds |
| Row H-292 | A write that went down drops the mark of every command the copy taken before it holds whose number still matches, so one outcome's write lets go of marks that are not its own. | Promote. How the mark is let go is the subject the scope puts first: N8-03 has the mark standing and no article has it going |
| Row H-293 | A write that raises enters no drop at all, so every mark stays. | Merge with H-292, the half that says what the bulk drop depends on |
| Row H-294 | The operator's write puts down the same whole state through the same store and drops no mark. | Merge with H-292. N8-03 has that write as a cause of its own; that it leaves every mark standing belongs with what does drop them |
| Row H-295 | A failed outcome write raises nothing out of the handling, so the call goes on and answers its caller. | Merge with H-292: the same failed-write road H-293 names, carried a step further — the handling is answered rather than stopped, so the marks left standing and the settled record withheld are what it leaves behind, and P2-41 states what the second of those costs. N8-03 has the operator's half of that contrast and limits it to the operator |
| Row H-296 | The settled record is asked for where the result succeeded and no write of its changes failed. | Decline. P2-41 states it, and states the case this row first got wrong |
| Row H-297 | The settled record's wait can run out with nothing calling the handed-over write off, so the record may land after the host stopped waiting. | Promote. P2-41 has that shape for the stopped-trying record and cites 3209-3215 for it; the settled record's own wait is cited nowhere |
| Row H-298 | Where the loop or the trading side is absent the settled record is not attempted at all. | Merge with H-297, the other way that record fails to go down |

N9-01 states that no host reaches that road and that it passes the squaring over; what belongs beside the start that weighs two nothings is that nothing below that road runs at all |
| Row H-302 | A holding Nexus has that Praxis does not report stops the start rather than being given up. | Promote. The seeded three-arm candidate names this refusal as an arm of its own and its sibling arms are each an article already — N3-10, N5-05 and N3-11 — so folding the stop into another subject would leave the one arm without a page. No article states it |
| Row H-303 | A holding Praxis reports that Nexus does not hold is taken in and counted, or turned away and counted for nothing. | Decline. N3-10 states it and N5-05 states the refusal's exclusion from the sum |
| Row H-304 | A holding both sides have takes Praxis's amount where the two differ. | Decline. N3-11 states it |
| Row H-305 | A holding whose basis stands at nothing is given a price, and that price is written onto it. | Decline. N3-11 states it |
| Row H-306 | The write is asked for only where the rebuilt total and the stored figure differ. | Decline. N7-08 states it |
| Row H-307 | A start that recovered nothing and is reported nothing it can take in leaves both figures at nothing, so the start with least to square is the one that squares against nothing. | Promote as the article holding what that squaring settles without writing. N7-08 has the rule the test applies and N9-01 has the checkpoint as one a start may make; which starts meet that test is in neither |
| Row H-308 | The road an arriving report takes turns it, does the in-memory accounting and queues it, reading no settled record between. | Promote. P2-41 and P2-39 have a start reading those records; that the live road reads none of them is in neither |
| Row H-309 | A report for an account with no queue is dropped where it arrives. | Merge with H-308 as the road on which that route itself drops a report, the turning it returns before dropping one too, where P2-41 states |
| Row H-310 | Where the account's wiring is not yet published the accounting is skipped and the report is queued regardless. | Merge with H-308. Where H-309 drops a report outright this one keeps it, the synchronous pass skipped and the queue holding it for the loop to account for, so the two roads part on whether anything is lost at all |
| Row H-311 | The gather of settled names is reached from one call inside the build, itself reached from one line, both ahead of every loop that build makes. | Merge with H-308: what makes the live road's silence a finding is that the only reader runs before those loops are built |
| Row H-312 | The store is built without the guarded pair, and makes its own log lock on the way. | Merge with H-313, which is what that absence costs |
| Row H-313 | The start runs before the pair exists, so the one checkpoint it may ask for writes with the guard taking neither lock. | Promote. N7-08 has that checkpoint as one of three callers and N9-01 has which hosts reach it; that it runs unguarded is in neither |
| Row H-314 | The pair is handed over once the start has returned, and a second handover is refused, as is anything that is not the pair. | Merge with H-313. This handover ends the window H-312 opens, so the three rows are that window's two ends and what falls inside it |
| Row H-315 | The strategy-event write takes the log lock alone inside the store, its caller holding the first half of the pair, so the second half is held by nothing on that road. | Promote. A separate subject from the window: which of the store's three writes runs under what, rather than when the pair is missing |

Seven subjects promote, where the package-9 shape was five, and two rows account
for the difference. H-315 is one: the window in which the pair is absent and the
question of which write runs under which lock were one row at the Seed and will
not fit one article. If the lock article outgrows the cap as the third write is
traced, the split is pre-authorised along the same seam — the window keeps the
start, and which writes take what becomes its own page. H-302 is the other, and
it promoted on review rather than on the plan: its sibling arms each hold a page,
so the refusal that stops a start cannot be the one arm folded into a neighbour.

Eight rows decline, every one of them to an article that already states the
claim, and four of those eight belong to the squaring. That is the measure of
how much of candidate three was already written: of the seven rows traced for
it, four were N3-10's, N3-11's and N7-08's before this package began, and a fifth
is half N9-01's.

How the mark is let go is the subject that must come first. The other six can be
read in any order, but each of the three that touch the store's writes assumes
the mark article's account of which road asks for a write at all.

## Package 6 — what one road leaves standing, and what another waits on

Scope: the marks a write leaves standing, and the write a later outcome asks
for on one of them; the two lock histories behind one write to the log, and the
roads that never reach it; and what a write of the whole state waits on once
the locks covering it are attached. The four candidates the package-5 re-seed
named, taken through the stages together. This is the sixth package on the
Praxis side, and each of its subjects came out of verifying the fifth rather
than out of a module with no page.

The marks a write leaves standing is the subject that must come first. P5-01
has the copy a write takes before it, the walk over that copy after it, and a
command re-marked in between keeping its mark on the number it carries; the
copy is read there as holding every mark then standing. A command marked for
the first time while the copy is already taken stands in no copy at all, so the
walk never reaches it, and nothing about what that write put down of the
command's figures comes into it. A mark is earned by either of the two marks a
result carries, and there are roads that carry each of them alone; the mark is
set once the accounting has returned, holding neither of the locks the write's
own guard takes. What the copy held is the whole of what the walk can reach.

### Merge and promote, what one road leaves standing

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-316 | The mapping the marks stand in is added to in one place and deleted from in one place, so nothing else in Praxis lets a mark go. | Merge with H-319. That the only deletion is the walk's is what makes a mark outside the copy durable rather than merely unvisited |
| Row H-317 | The accounting that earns a mark has returned before the mark is set, and the mark is set under a lock the accounting ran without. | Merge with H-319, as half of why the two acquisitions are unordered against each other |
| Row H-318 | The copy a write walks is taken under that same lock, and the lock is given up before the write runs. | Merge with H-319, the other half of the same |
| Row H-319 | The walk iterates the copy's own keys, so a command absent from the copy has no test run for it at all; P5-01 reaches the marks the copy holds and not the ones it does not. | Promote. P5-01 reads the copy as holding every mark then standing, so the marks it does not hold are in neither it nor N8-03 |
| Row H-320 | The write puts down the state as it stands when the serialize runs, and that state is the same object the accounting mutates. | Merge with H-319. It settles what a write puts down — the state as it stands, not a copy of it — so a mark outside the copy may stand for figures already down or for figures not yet made, and the walk tells neither from the other |
| Row H-321 | A mark is earned by either of the two marks a result carries, and there are roads that carry each of them alone. | Merge with H-319. The walk reads neither mark: it compares the number standing against a command with the number the copy holds, so a road that earned its entry by the money and one that earned it by the position are the same thing to it |
| Row H-336 | The mark is set holding neither of the locks the write's guard takes, the accounting having returned before it. | Merge with H-319. It closes the last reading on which the copy and the mark could be ordered by a lock |
| Row H-322 | The write an operator asks for puts the whole state down through the same store and drops no mark. | Decline across two: P5-01 states that it drops none, and N8-03 states that every such write puts the whole state out |
| Row H-323 | A later outcome for a marked command reads the mark where it stands, and the write is asked for where the result succeeded and that mark or one of the two the result carries is set. | Decline. N8-03 states it, the third of the three marks that ask for a write included |
| Row H-335 | A repeat of an outcome already worked through answers with a success carrying neither of the two marks the result holds, so a mark standing against its command is the whole of the reason a write is asked for. | Promote. P5-01 has the write that drops no mark and N8-03 has the mark that asks for a write; the road on which a standing mark is the only reason left is in neither |
| Row H-337 | The figures such a write puts down may be down already, the write an operator asked for having put the whole state down while leaving every mark standing. | Merge with H-335, which is what the second write is asked for; this is what there is left for it to put down |
| Row H-324 | The walk can drop the mark of the very command whose outcome asked for the write, the keys it tests being tested on the number alone, so a mark read later may have been let go by that command's own earlier outcome. | Merge with H-335. P5-01 has the drop going over every command the copy holds whose number matches; that a later read of the same command can find its own mark gone is the other thing that can meet, or fail, the test H-335 turns on |
| Row H-325 | The write of a strategy event is reached with the capital lock taken and given up again on one road, and with it never taken at all on another, the difference being whether a release was worked out for the exit at all. | Promote. P5-07 has which locks that write itself enters; what was held and given up before it is reached, and on which roads, is nowhere |
| Row H-326 | The roads that hand back nothing and still reach that write are two: a holding whose basis stands at nought, and one belonging to another strategy. | Merge with H-325. N2-01 has both roads handing back nothing and neither article has them reaching the event write, which is the half that belongs to the lock history |
| Row H-327 | A fill larger than the holding is turned back at the basis step and then raises at the position step, so that road never reaches the write at all. | Merge with H-325 as the road that never arrives. N3-07 has the raise and has such a fill getting no release worked out for it, and N2-01 has the two basis tests in order for a nought-basis holding alone; that the raise stands between the fill and the event write is in neither |
| Row H-328 | A release the controller refuses answers the caller before the write is reached either. | Merge with H-325, the second road that never arrives. N2-01 has the return and the failure it ends as, and says nothing of the write |
| Row H-329 | The write itself takes the log lock alone, its caller holding the lock a check where the pair is built requires to be the positions lock. | Merge with H-325. P5-07 has the write entering no guard and the check on what the pair may hold, and N3-03 has the caller holding its lock across the write; that the lock held is the one that check requires is in neither |
| Row H-330 | The lock the pair's second half is is the controller's own, made once as it is built and handed out by a reading that returns it. | Merge with H-331, which is what having one lock for both purposes costs |
| Row H-331 | Ten of the controller's own roads take that lock, so a write of the whole state waits on whichever of them is under way. | Promote. P5-06 has when the pair arrives and P5-07 has what the store's writes take; what else takes the same lock, and so what a write waits on, is in neither |
| Row H-332 | Five of the reserving road's refusals are raised before the lock is taken at all; once taken it is held across the purge of what has expired and whichever ending is reached. | Merge with H-331 as the measure of how long one of those ten holds it |
| Row H-333 | That road asks for no store write of any kind, and outside the tests it is reached from one line, the validator's capital stage. | Merge with H-331. A road that writes nothing itself still holds a write out, which is what makes the waiting worth a page |
| Row H-334 | The write waits on that lock only once the pair is attached; before then the guard takes nothing. | Merge with H-331. P5-06 has the pair arriving late and a start's checkpoint running with none in hand; the guard's own branch, taking nothing where there is no pair, and a write waiting where there is one, is nowhere |

Four subjects promote, one for each candidate the re-seed named, and the two
kinds it sorted them into stayed apart: neither gap inside one road's own
handling folded into either lock or mark held while another road reads it.

Sixteen rows merge and two decline, where the Seed had marked eight as written
already. Six of those eight moved. Three moved on one reading: an article that
owns what a road does to the money or the position does not thereby own what that
road reaches afterwards. N2-01 has a nought basis working out no release; it does
not have that road arriving at the event write. N3-07 has an oversize fill
raising; it does not have that raise standing between the fill and the write.
N2-01 has a refused release returning and ending as a failure, and says nothing
of the write at all. H-329 moved on lock identity instead: P5-07 has the write
entering no guard and N3-03 has its caller holding a lock across it, and that the
lock held is the one the pair check requires is in neither. H-324 moved on the
mark side: P5-01 has the drop going over every command the copy holds whose number
matches, and not that a later read of the same command can find its own mark gone.
H-334 moved on the guard's own branch: P5-06 has the pair arriving late and a
start's checkpoint running with none in hand, and not the guard taking nothing
where there is no pair. Of the two that stay declined, one is where the Seed put
it and the other is split across two articles instead of the one it named.
Candidate two so leaves with all five of its rows in one article rather than one
row and four duplicates.

Candidate one came in as a mark the walk never reaches and leaves as the whole of
what the walk can reach, seven rows deep. Three of the six that merge into it are
orderings between the copy and the mark that had to be ruled out one at a time;
the other three are that the walk's deletion is the only one anywhere, that the
serialize reads the live state rather than a copy, and that either of the two
marks a result carries earns an entry the walk cannot tell from the other.

The Seed said what the walk cannot reach must come first, and this stage
withdraws that. The write a standing mark asks for rests on P5-01's write that
drops none rather than on a mark outside any copy, and the two lock subjects do
not touch the marks at all. Nothing here depends on anything else here. The four
are numbered in pairs rather than in the order the candidates were named: the two
that turn on a mark first, then the two that turn on a lock.

Three of the four promoted subjects carry an article. The fourth, the write a
standing mark can ask for, carries none and holds no row below: every claim drafted for
it turned out to be written already — the test's third arm at N8-03, and the
acknowledgement that sets neither of the other two at the article on what an
entry outcome changes. What remained was a consequence of those two with nothing
of its own to cite, and the brief refuses a page built that way. The finding
stands in this register and in no article.

What the money's lock is doing when a result is written was renamed twice at the
writing, as what it could carry was settled. Of the five rows behind it,
three state what a road does to the money or the position, which N2-01 and N3-07
carry between them, and a fourth states what the write itself takes and what its
caller holds, which P5-07 and N3-03 carry apart from the one clause neither has,
that the lock held is the pair's own first half; that clause is re-seeded below
rather than written here. The fifth is what no article has, in
two parts: that the lock over the money is given back before the write rather
than held across it, and that the roads working out no release reach that write
with it never taken. So the two-histories shape the Merge promoted it for
stands.

It was nearly lost. A reading partway through the writing struck the second of
those two parts as N3-07's and cut the article to the first alone. N2-01 has the
roads that work out no release, and N3-07 has the roads that raise instead and so
never arrive at all; neither names the write the first of those reaches, and
the two articles that had cited that call before this package, N3-03 and N1-07, name neither of those roads as reaching it. The claim
was the article's all along, and is back.

## Spines

A spine is a numbered walkthrough sequencing articles end to end, with every
fork named. Each sentence hands off to the article that owns its behaviour, and
the route itself — the order those behaviours happen in, which no article owns —
is proved by the spine's own evidence against the same pinned commits an article
cites. The check enforces both.

| ID | Route | Status | Spine |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-01 | Cancelling a trade, with every fork it can leave by | written | [cancelling-a-trade.md](spines/cancelling-a-trade.md) |
| S-02 | Draining an account's queues, one pass end to end | written | [draining-an-accounts-queues.md](spines/draining-an-accounts-queues.md) |
| S-03 | Changing work already sent, one arm per way of working | written | [changing-work-already-sent.md](spines/changing-work-already-sent.md) |
| S-04 | Stopping a host with work in flight | written | [stopping-a-host-with-work-in-flight.md](spines/stopping-a-host-with-work-in-flight.md) |
| S-05 | Starting a host back up, to ready or gated | written | [starting-a-host-back-up.md](spines/starting-a-host-back-up.md) |

These are the five the brief names. Each was walked against the code rather than
assembled from the articles, after the first draft of S-01 was rejected for
sequencing articles instead of the dispatch.

## Seeded for the Praxis remainder

What two independent reviews of this package found still uncovered on the Praxis
side. The three subjects listed here are now written, as P4-59 to P4-61; what
remains is the branches below.

The four branches this list carried are written too: the ladder amend's three
refusals in P4-47, the not-active refusal on a protection re-price in P4-48,
the watch returning before its deadline in P4-42, and squaring an order making
no filled ending of its own in P4-55. That left nothing on the Praxis side seeded and
unwritten, and the packages went to the Nexus side below; package 7, seeded after
those, is the Praxis side again.

## Package 7 — the modules written around

Scope: the four modules the Praxis side marks uncovered, which the wiki has
written around, and the Nexus module that does the Praxis side's logging over
again — and, for each, what it checks and what it does not. Three of the four were
written around by tracing their behaviour through a caller; the fourth has no
caller to trace, nothing outside the tests building it at all. A fill's own record, the name a bracket's protective exit is
given, the setting up of logging, and three records that refuse nothing.

Closing these leaves the Praxis side with no module marked uncovered. Six
Nexus modules have no check written in them. Two of the six are sets of named
values, and a value outside one of those sets is refused by the machinery the
set is built on rather than by anything in the module. The six are: the named
values in `nexus/core/domain/enums.py` and `nexus/core/stp_mode.py`, the two
shapes a connector must answer to in
`nexus/infrastructure/praxis_connector/inbound_connector.py` and
`outbound_connector.py`, the record of how a lifecycle call went in
`nexus/core/capital_controller/lifecycle_result.py`, and the error raised at
startup in `nexus/startup/error.py`. This package names them so the ledger can
say what they are rather than leaving them counted. Twelve Nexus modules stay
`partial` by citation after that, and the ledger goes on calling those work to
do.

Six subjects promote, seven rows merge, four are recorded as already written, and one is recorded as an exclusion, as
package 6 did with two. All four are the same shape: a statement identical on the
two sides, or identical between a record and the event carrying it, with an article
already holding one copy.

A fill's own record comes first and takes three subjects, because it is the only
one of the five with refusals of its own: what it requires, what it never asks, and
that none of it runs. The name a bracket's exit is given comes next, for the
derivation its own module calls a contract between the two repositories while only
one of them carries it. The Nexus logging gets one subject,
for its single difference from the Praxis arrangement already written up, and the
three records that refuse nothing get one between them.

### Re-seed, after writing package 7

What seven rounds of verifying the six articles turned up that none of them
reaches. Each was checked against the index and the ledger before it was written
down here.

| Candidate | What proves it |
|---|---|
| What the figures a fill carries are refused for, and what gets past by raising instead | `praxis/core/domain/fill.py` 74, 77 and 80 compare three figures with nought and ask nothing else of them, so a figure standing at infinity is kept. A figure that is not a number at all does not leave by the same door: under the context Python traps that comparison in by default, the comparison itself raises rather than answering, so the record is turned back by a different error than every other refusal it makes, and from a line written to catch something else. Where that trap is lifted, the comparison answers false and the figure is kept. P7-02 has what the three checks ask; that one class of bad figure leaves by another door is in no article |

One candidate, where three were drafted. The other two were struck on reading the
articles they were supposed to extend. That a fill's own record and the event
standing for the same fill require different sets of names is already in P7-01,
which permits the empty venue trade name in one and requires every name in the
other, citing both. Which keys the scoped binding puts back is already in P7-05,
which has the key rebound inside restored and the key bound inside under another
name left standing. Both had been drafted from the same citations the articles
carry, which is what makes this kind of candidate easy to write and worthless: the
evidence was already spent.

Verifying this package cost nine rounds, and the familiar class accounted for most
of them: prose outrunning its citations. A claim about the
event record's names that the article's own citations did not carry. A handover to
the other side that nothing cited. Three statements of what a figure must be,
written as an inequality the code does not make.

That last is a class worth naming: a comparison written as a requirement. "Must
stand above nought" and "is refused at or below nought" turn the same figures away,
so the first is not wrong about what gets in. It is wrong about the statement doing
the work. The line compares and does not require, and for a figure that is not a
number it raises rather than answering under the default trapping context, so such
a record is turned back by a different error and from a line written to catch
something else. Three statements of
three figures' rules were written the first way and had to be written the second.
The reviewer found it by running the pinned record on such a figure, which is the
second package in a row where executing the class rather than reading it is what
found the defect.

One finding was misplaced and then put back. The note that a module's docstring
claims a contract the code does not keep was taken out of its article, because the
gate refuses a docstring as evidence, and recorded in the merge disposition
instead. That was wrong about the gate: `scripts/check_wiki.py` 5269-5270 accepts a
docstring citation where the claim itself says it is about a docstring, which is
`_claims_a_docstring` at 4643. The finding belongs in the article, cites the
docstring, and is back there. A rule the gate carries is worth reading before
working around it.

## Nexus package 1 — Seed output

Approved for tracing, not a claim of completeness. The reviewer challenged the
candidate list, added one subject it missed, narrowed one that was too broad, and
set the order below as a tracing order rather than a claim that these five things
happen in sequence. Pinned at Nexus `953477a`; paths below are under `nexus/`.

| Order | Topic | The bounded question, and why here |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The checks a decision passes | What decides which checks run, where validation stops, and what an allowed decision carries out of it — `core/validator/pipeline_executor.py` 45-78 and 162-176, `core/validator/pipeline_models.py` 50-57, with abort actions taking a separate road at `strategy/action_submit.py` 201-203. First, because it establishes the route the rest hang off |
| 2 | How Nexus sets money aside | Already written as N1-01, retained with its scope. It gives the reservation the checks carry out of them a meaning |
| 3 | What Nexus does when a command does not get across | Already written as N1-02, retained. Traced after the reservation so what is released or kept has a defined object |
| 4 | Outcomes waiting on a strategy | An outcome that cannot yet be matched to a strategy is parked, later outcomes for that command queue behind it, and the queue is drained or discarded — `core/outcome_loop.py` 209-231, 278-304 and 327-343. The subject the candidate list missed: the handover is hard to follow without the return-side race |
| 5 | What an entry outcome changes | How an acknowledgement, a fill, a refusal, a cancellation and an expiry each move an entry's tracked capital, and when its position grows — `infrastructure/praxis_connector/outcome_processor.py` 191-209, 224-239, 256-258, 305-355 and 473-490 |

Both already-written topics survived the challenge with their scope intact. They
entered out of order, which the note below records; the fix is the package's
boundaries, not a retrospective case for having picked them.

### Merge and promote, topic one

The one-sentence test on each harvest row from tracing the checks: merge where one
sentence expresses alternative conditions for the same answer, split where it must
answer an additional independent question.

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-071 | The checks run in a configured order, which by default is intake, risk, price, capital, health, platform limits. | Promoted into N1-03, which states the list and that it is given rather than fixed |
| Row H-072 | For an exit, an abort, a cancel or a change, five of the six are skipped and only intake runs. | Merged into N1-03 with H-071, one sentence with alternative conditions for the same answer |
| Row H-073 | A stage answering with the wrong shape, or denying in another stage's name, raises rather than passing. | Promoted, then split out to N1-04 at the verify stage: the shape an answer must take is an additional independent question, and the trace had missed that a refusal must name a stage, a reason and a message while an allowance carries none |
| Row H-074 | A denial carries forward a reservation an earlier stage granted, where it has none of its own. | Promoted into N1-03, which states it as the reason a refusal has something to give back |
| Row H-075 | An abort reaches Praxis by a road that does not use this result at all. | Queue as its own candidate. "And also, how does an abort get there?" is an additional independent question, so the test splits it |

The topic was written as one article and came back from verify at 453 words,
which the cap makes a split rather than a trim. N1-03 keeps the order, the
skipping and what a decision carries out; N1-04 takes the shape an answer
must have. H-075 went to the queue below rather than widening either.

| Equity peaks and drawdown | `core/domain/risk_state.py` 238-270 keeps separate peaks and derives both the current and the worst drawdown |
| Losses counted over rolling windows | `infrastructure/loss_derivation.py` 39-45 and 87-121 sum only negative results into three windows, profits never offsetting them; `infrastructure/state_store.py` 377-390 refreshes and clears them |
| Nexus state recovered from its own record | `infrastructure/state_store.py` 259-274 and 286-333, `infrastructure/snapshot.py` 45-53, and `infrastructure/wal.py` 200-220 stopping at a damaged record |
| Stopping Nexus strategies | `startup/shutdown_sequencer.py` 183-196, 343-386 permitting only exits and aborts, and 614-627 escalating what is still pending |
| Exit ownership and what is left to sell | `core/validator/intake_stage.py` 237-285 requires a position the asking strategy owns and limits the exit by what is already pending |

Tracing was expected to turn up more, and the re-seed below records what it did:
exit accounting, dust and fund movements, and the capital lifecycle past its
first reservation each arrived with evidence. The detailed admission rules,
persistence across a restart and trading permission are still expected. Each
enters this queue with its evidence. None of them enlarges this package, and a topic that will not fit
the template comes back as a narrower five rather than a sixth.

Promised by both articles here that stop at the entry, and the larger half of the subject |

The exit subject is the one that must come first: two articles in this package
say a figure waits for the exit, and until it is written those sentences point
nowhere.

## Reader's pass over packages thirteen to eighteen

One of the two external readers came back after fourteen packages written without
one. It was given the ten articles those packages added, the pinned revisions of
both repositories, and one instruction: report statements that are false,
overclaimed or unsupported, and nothing else. It returned fourteen reports over
eight of the ten articles; two came back clean. Every report held up on reading
the pinned source again, and every one is acted on below.

| What it found | What came of it |
|---|---|
| The day a bar is filed under rested on a zone no caller can supply. The one thing that works these figures out builds every moment in universal time from the bar's own stamp, so the zone the article turned on is fixed before the figures see it. | P15-03 is rewritten around what is true: which of a bar's two moments the day is taken from, the stamp rather than the close, and the window having been filtered on the close. The candidate the twelfth package's re-seed raised is corrected in place |
| A bar with an unknown price is not left out of everything. It keeps its place, so a held run it falls inside is counted as two trades, each charged for an opening and a closing. | P15-01 is rewritten with a section of its own for what an unknown price still does. Running the pinned masks confirms two openings and two closings where one bar's price is missing |
| Reading the series turns back text that is no number at all, rather than making it not-a-number. Only a value with no numeric form of its own becomes that. | P15-01 now separates the two: the reading refuses the first before any check runs, and the second parts company between the predictions and the prices |
| Cancelling at shutdown reaches what is still queued at that moment, not everything accepted while a replay ran. | P14-02 now says so, and turns on where the worker has got to |
| A pair of edges with no zone in reverse order is refused by the ordering, not by the frames. | P14-01 now says a pair in order goes on to the frames |
| A backfill with no mark sends a starting moment only where it was given one, the window's far edge is open but the pass still ends where the pages run out, and a mark is set only by a full page of readable names. | P16-01 carries all three corrections |
| An even split with a count carrying a fraction is refused by the arithmetic, which may be the division or the building of the list; a lot size given as a whole number is not refused at all; and the children do not divide a total exactly where the total carries more digits than the exact kind's precision. | P16-02 carries all three corrections |
| A comparison with nought does not let an infinity or a figure that is no number through where the figure is of the exact kind, since such a comparison raises. | P13-01 now says where the figure is an ordinary number |
| Swept from the last of those: one other article claimed an even split comes to its total exactly. | P10-03 carries the same correction, with the precision that bounds it, and is where P16-02 now sends a reader for what the division leaves over |

What the classes say about the method. Two of the eight are the class the
register's own rule names — a claim true of a function and false on the only path
that reaches it — and both were written by a session that had read the function
and not its caller. The rest are a step past the citation: a word stronger than
the line supports, or a second clause the first clause does not carry.

No blocking check comes out of this. The path class would need the gate to know
which callers a module has and which claims speak about inputs, and neither is
expressible in what the gate reads; the overclaiming class was measured against
the clean corpus twice before and both candidate checks died there. What is
expressible is already written: the gate's refusal of a citation that stops before
the raise, and its refusal of two mutually exclusive branches paired as a
sequence, both of which held through this pass.

A second round went to the same reader, over the statements the first round had
not covered: the six added to existing articles by the seventeenth and eighteenth
packages, one extended citation, and the article split out during the first
round's corrections. Five came back clean. Two did not.

| What it found | What came of it |
|---|---|
| The moment behind the rolling loss windows is not asked merely for a zone: the zone must give an offset for that moment. | N15-08 now says so, which is the wording the other articles making this test already use |
| The article split out in the first round was wrong three ways over. The weights are used, not merely counted; a weight below nought is caught only among the weights that are read, the last never being examined; and on the two roads that reach the split, a weight below nought cannot arrive at all, both records that carry weights in demanding finite figures of the exact kind above nought, adding to one. | P18-02 is rewritten around what is true and reachable: the split asks nothing of the weights, the records that carry them in ask everything, and the last weight is never read because the last child is the remainder — the adding-to-one rule being what makes those the same share. Its title says so too |

The second finding is the path class again, and this time the session wrote it
while correcting the path class. That is the measure of how little the gate can
see of it: three of the sixteen reports over these two rounds were claims about
inputs a caller cannot supply, and none of the three was visible in the file the
claim cited.

The packages written without a reader are therefore no longer unread. The other
reader is still unavailable, its balance exhausted.

So a caller handing in a lag longer than the history, or bars with no prices in them, is answered rather than refused, and the answer is indistinguishable from a history in which nothing was ever held. Scope: the service that takes a replay request over HTTP, which the ledger marks
`full` with seventeen of its seventy-three statements uncited and is the widest
such module left. Three articles reach it: what the request must carry, what a run
reports back, and where its bars come from. How the two edges of the window are
read, and what becomes of a queued run when the listener stops, are in no article.

### Merge and promote, package 14

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-546 | A moment ending in a capital Z is rewritten to a zero offset and any other text goes to the reader as it stands, so a moment with no zone is kept and a lower-case z is refused. | Promote. What a request's two edges may say is in no article, and the rewriting of one letter is the whole of what the service does to them |
| Row H-547 | One edge carrying a zone and one not is refused by the comparison between them, before the bars are asked for. | Merge with H-546. Which refusal catches a mixed pair is the half a reader would get wrong, since the refusal they would expect sits further on |
| Row H-549 | Every failure in building the scenario comes back as a bad-request answer carrying the raiser's own words. | Merge with H-546. How these refusals reach the asker is what makes the distinction between them visible from outside |
| Row H-548 | Both edges without a zone are refused by the loader that reads the frames. | Recorded; the article on where a replay's bars come from states it |
| Row H-551 | On the app's cleanup the worker pool is shut down, waiting for the replay still running and cancelling those queued behind it. | Promote. What stopping the listener does to work already accepted is in no article, and the two halves of that one call pull in opposite directions |
| Row H-552 | A record is set to running where the work is queued, and only the worker's own function ever writes it again. | Merge with H-551. A cancelled run's record standing at running for good is what the shutdown leaves behind, and it reads only beside the shutdown |
| Row H-550 | Runs go to a pool of one worker, made as the app is. | Recorded; the article on what a run reports back states it |

Two subjects promote, two rows merge into each, and two are recorded as already
stated.

The moments come first, because a request is read before anything is queued.
Stopping comes second.

ledger marks `full` with sixteen of its thirty-three statements uncited. The

## Package 12 — how a replay's figures are counted

Scope: the three helpers inside the module that reproduces the training side's own
distribution figures, which the ledger marks `full` with forty-eight of its hundred
statements uncited. The three are what counts as one trade, how the bars are
grouped into days, and how a fall from a peak is recorded.

Each of the three answers nothing where there is nothing to count, and each has one
case a reader would get wrong: a trade's return is compounded rather than summed, a
day holding no position reports no return for its exposure rather than nought, and
a fall never recovered leaves the depths and the lengths of unequal number.

### Merge and promote, package 12

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-531 | The bars are numbered into runs by a running count of the entry marks, and one trade is the held bars sharing a number. | Promote. What the figures count as one trade is in no article, and it is the unit every per-trade figure rests on |
| Row H-530 | Where no bar is marked as an entry, no trade is counted at all. | Merge with H-531, the answer where there is nothing to number |
| Row H-532 | A trade's return is its bars' returns multiplied together rather than added, so two bars of a tenth give just over a fifth. | Merge with H-531. How a run's bars combine is the half of the unit that a reader would get wrong, and it reads only beside what the run is |
| Row H-534 | The evaluated bars are grouped by the day their moment falls in, and the days are reported in order. | Promote. How the bars are cut into days, and what a day with no position reports, are in no article |
| Row H-533 | Where no bar is evaluated the grouping stays empty and no window is reported. | Merge with H-534, the answer where there is nothing to group |
| Row H-535 | A day's return is its bars' returns multiplied together, given in basis points. | Merge with H-534, the first of the two figures each day carries |
| Row H-536 | A day's return for each unit of exposure is that return over the share of its bars holding a position, and is given as nothing at all where no bar held one. | Merge with H-534. The second figure, and the one that answers nothing rather than nought, which is what a reader averaging the days would need to know |
| Row H-539 | An episode opens on the first fall below the peak, deepens while the fall continues, and closes on the first recovery, recording its depth in basis points and its length in days. | Promote. How a fall from a peak is recorded is in no article, and the three arms of its one test are what make an episode |
| Row H-537 | Where nothing is evaluated, no drawdown episode is counted. | Merge with H-539, the answer where there is nothing to walk |
| Row H-538 | A peak is kept that only rises, and each fall is measured against it. | Merge with H-539, what the falls are measured from |
| Row H-540 | An episode still open at the end records its depth and no length, so the depths and the lengths need not come to the same number. | Promote. Two lists a reader would pair off that need not be of equal number is a subject of its own, and the reason is the one episode that never closed |

Four subjects promote and seven rows merge, with nothing declined.

The trade comes first, because the per-trade figures rest on it. The days come
second, as the other way the same bars are cut. The episodes come third. The
unequal lists are held out of the episodes because a reader pairing a depth with a
length is the one who is misled, and that reader may never read as far as how an
episode opens.

## Package 11 — the poller that keeps the book, and the stop that waits for it

Scope: the poller that keeps the order book fresh, of which the tick and the
swallowed failure are cited and nothing else is, together with the path the host
takes to stop its pollers.

Four articles reach this module for what a tick does and for the failure it
swallows. What it refuses, what it answers about itself, how the gap between polls
is actually waited out, and that its stop waits for the work in hand are in no
article.

### Merge and promote, package 11

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-522 | A poller refuses an empty symbol and an interval at or below nought, both by comparison and neither by type. | Promote. What the poller refuses and what it answers about itself are in no article, and both are read by the host that drives it |
| Row H-523 | It answers that it is running where its task exists and has not finished. | Merge with H-522. The answer is what the refusal below turns on, so the two read together |
| Row H-524 | Starting one that is already running is refused. | Merge with H-522, the refusal that reads the answer above |
| Row H-525 | Starting clears the stop signal and makes a task named for the symbol. | Merge with H-522. Clearing the signal before the task exists is what lets a stopped poller be started again, which the refusal alone would not show |
| Row H-526 | The gap between polls is a wait on the stop signal bounded by the interval, so the waiting ends either when the signal comes or when the interval runs out. | Promote. The gap is written as a bounded wait rather than a sleep, which means a stop is noticed at once instead of after the interval, and no article says so |
| Row H-527 | Stopping sets the signal, cancels the task, waits for it while swallowing the cancellation, and then forgets it. | Promote. The article on what a loop's stop leaves running has four stops that wait for nothing and one that waits; this is a second that waits, and its own shape is in no article |
| Row H-528 | The host stops its pollers one after another, each waited for, the whole bounded at ten seconds. | Merge with H-527. The poller's own stop and the host's walk over all of them are one subject: the bound on the whole is what the waiting costs |
| Row H-529 | A stop that fails or runs out of time keeps the pollers recorded for another attempt, where one that finishes forgets them. | Merge with H-527. What a failed stop leaves behind is the far side of a stop that waits, since a stop that waited for nothing would have nothing to leave |

Three subjects promote and five rows merge, with nothing declined.

What the poller refuses and answers comes first, because the host reads both. The
gap between polls comes second, as the one thing about this loop that differs from
the others in shape rather than in detail. The stop comes last and takes the host's
walk with it, because the bound on that walk is what waiting for each poller costs.

A note on a mistake made and corrected while committing this package. The gate
wrapper used all session lifts another session's uncommitted release section out of
the changelog, runs the check, and puts it back. Staging the changelog straight
after a gate run therefore captured that section, and one commit carried eight
lines of another branch's work. The commit after it removes them, and the staging
path is now a wrapper that lifts the section, refuses outright if it is still
staged, commits, and restores — so the mistake cannot be made again rather than
being remembered against.

four ticks follow four failures with the loop still going, and that a stop part way
through a gap of a twentieth of a second returns in a time too small to measure.
The articles carry status `written`, as 296 of the 297 do.

## Package 10 — what a ladder insists on, and what a lot step leaves behind

Scope: the settings for orders spread across several prices, of which one check is
cited and six are not, and one consequence of how an even split rounds that the
article on timed work states the halves of without joining.

The article on orders at several prices has that there must be at least two and
that weights split the amount. What those prices must be, and what those weights
must come to, is in no article.

| Row H-515 | Each price must be a positive finite decimal. | Merge with H-514, the check made of each price before the order among them is weighed |
| Row H-516 | A ladder may descend as well as rise, either answer satisfying the test. | Merge with H-514. That the test takes either direction is what makes it an ordering rule rather than a rising one, and it reads only beside the rule itself |
| Row H-517 | Weights may be left out, and the checking stops there where they are. | Promote. What a ladder's weights must come to is in no article, and the sum they must reach is the sharpest of the four checks on them |
| Row H-518 | Weights given must be a tuple of the same length as the prices. | Merge with H-517, the first of the three checks made where weights were given |
| Row H-519 | Each weight must be a positive finite decimal. | Merge with H-517, the second |
| Row H-520 | The weights must come to exactly one, so a set short by a hundredth is refused. | Merge with H-517. It is the one check reading the weights together rather than one at a time, and the reason the others are not enough |
| Row H-521 | Where a lot step is known the last slice is floored to it as the others are, so the slices can come to less than the total asked for and the remainder is never sent. | Promote. The article on timed work has the last slice taking what is left and has each slice floored to the step; that the two together mean the total sent can fall short is in no article |

Three subjects promote and five rows merge, with nothing declined.

The prices come first and the weights second, in the order the checks run, with
the sum the weights must reach held inside the weights rather than apart from them
because it is the reason the other three do not suffice. The quantity a step leaves
behind comes last, and belongs to this package rather than to the article it
corrects because it is a consequence of two facts that article already carries.

| Row H-508 | A bucket of permissions refuses a rate at or below nought and a capacity at or below nought. | Promote. What the bucket that paces calls to the venue refuses, and how it refills, is in no article |

Four subjects promote and six rows merge, with nothing declined.

The record comes first with what it refuses, and the copy across the seam second.
The bucket follows: what it refuses and how it refills, then the taking that
happens before the waiting and what is put back when the waiting is cut short.

A note on something the comparison turned up and the articles do not claim. The
two copies of the health report differ in the order of the two conditions in the
failure-count check: one asks for a true-or-false first and the other for a whole
number first. Either order turns a true-or-false back, because the test is an
or-chain satisfied by whichever condition holds, so the difference changes nothing
and the article says so rather than presenting it as a divergence.

waits exactly — four takes from a bucket of two leave it at minus two, with waits
of one second and two — and what showed the refund returning the count to nought
rather than to one. The articles carry status `written`, as 290 of the 291 do.

## Package 8 — what the records answer, and the checks nothing reached

Scope: the behaviour left uncited in modules the ledger already marks `full`, found
by parsing each module and comparing the statements inside its function bodies
against the lines the articles cite. Twenty-nine modules carry such a gap. This
package takes the eight smallest, where the whole module can be read at once and
the gap is a question the record answers or a check nothing reached.

| Row H-496 | A command answers whether it was sized in quote units by whether it carries a quote amount at all. | Merge with H-495, the second of the four and the first of two asking it the same way |
| Row H-497 | An order answers that same question the same way. | Merge with H-495. The command and the order answer by the same one-line test, which is what makes the pair worth reading together |
| Row H-498 | A position answers that it is closed where its amount stands equal to nought. | Merge with H-495, the fourth, and the one whose test is an equality rather than a presence |

| Row H-501 | A replay clock refuses a start carrying no zone, and keeps the one it is given. | Promote. What a clock refuses, and what one stands in where none was given, is in no article |

The questions the records answer come first, because four records share the shape
and nothing states any of them. The shared check for a name follows, with what it

one standing in. The severity handed back comes last.

A note on the method, and on something it turned up about the ledger itself.
The gap map was rebuilt for this package to parse each module and take only the
statements inside its function bodies, which cut the list of modules marked `full`
with an uncited statement from fifty to twenty-nine: the earlier count was
inflated by multi-line field declarations, type unions, signatures and the
continuation lines of `__all__`, none of which an article should have to cite.
Twenty-two remain after this package. The next ones by size are
`praxis/core/generate_client_order_id.py` at 5 of 30, `praxis/infrastructure/book_cache.py`
at 6 of 41, `praxis/core/domain/bracket_modify.py` at 7 of 23, and
`praxis/core/domain/health_snapshot.py` with every one of its 29 uncited.

Resyncing the ledger's own cells against the rows that cite each file corrected
nineteen of them. Those had fallen behind as packages 15 to 18 added articles
citing files whose cells nobody updated — the gate catches a cell naming a row
that does not cite the file, and catches a cell omitting a row, but only for the
files a package touches, so a cell can drift while every package passes. That is a
defect class worth a check of its own, and the check already exists: it simply was
not run over every row until now.

A note on verification, as for the four packages before it. The external readers
were still unavailable, so this package was checked by the gate, a reading of the
pinned source, and running the pinned records on the cases each claim turns on.
That is how the blank-space finding was found at all: the shared check was read as
trimming, as the records across the seam do, and running it on three spaces showed
it does not. The articles carry status `written`, as 286 of the 287 do.

article.

## Nexus package 18 — the last corners of the small records

Scope: the behaviour left uncited in thirteen small records on the Nexus side, found
by comparing every line an article cites against every line that runs. Most of what
the comparison turned up was a field being declared, which needs no article. Five
modules had real behaviour nothing reached: the record of how processing went, the
record of one entry in the log, a signal's way of answering for a key it lacks, how
the six kinds of outcome are cut into groups, and what the breaker limits are before
anything sets them.

Closing these lets thirteen modules move from `partial` to `full`. Four on this
side carry that mark for their own behaviour today, and six carry it as shapes.

| Row H-487 | The flag those two checks turn on is never asked to be a flag, nor are the two saying what was changed, so text in its place is read for truth alone. | Merge with H-485. The field both checks turn on being the one field whose type is never asked is what makes the pair worth reading |
| Row H-488 | Three kinds of entry are named for the log. | Promote. What one entry in the log refuses is in no article, the module being reached only through the reader that walks it |
| Row H-489 | An entry's number is refused below nought and asked nothing else, so a true-or-false or a fraction at or above nought is let by. | Merge with H-488. The number that orders the log being asked only for its sign is the sharpest of its four checks |

against the clean corpus, as the rule requires, and it was not written. The method

check — that a module marked `full` has every running line cited — it fires on fifty of the sixty-three

no article should have to cite; some is real. Either way the ledger's own bar is a
judgement, not line coverage, and a check reading it as coverage would refuse
almost every `full` row in the file. The method stays a way of finding work rather
than a gate.

behaviour either take a row or have their `full` corrected.

going to `full` would be the first on this side to carry that mark for behaviour,
where four already did; a sentence about what the log's reader does with a bad
number that nothing cited; and a flag said to send a record down the success arm
where an empty text sends it down the other one and is then kept for carrying a
reason. The articles carry status `written`, as 282 of the 283 do.

## Nexus package 17 — what a host is configured with, and what loads a strategy

Scope: the last two modules the ledger reaches only by citation — the record of
how one instance is configured, which carries twenty-seven refusals, and the pair
of calls that turn a line of a manifest into a running strategy. Thirty-eight refusals between them, and one line of the two cited by one
article.

No other record on this side does all three of what this one does: rewrite three
of its own fields, weigh one field against another, and sum across the entries of a
mapping. Three is more fields rewritten than any other record on this side
rewrites. The loader is the only place on this side that reads a file named from
outside and runs it.

What the record asks of its plain fields comes first, and the three things that
make it unlike anything else on this side follow: the mapping it accepts where its
neighbours accept less, the keys it rewrites and then refuses for clashing, and the
one check that reads a mapping's entries together. The field it rewrites over
itself comes after those, with the cross-field refusal it exists for. The loader
takes the last two subjects, split where its work does: what it asks of the path,
and what it asks of what the path turned out to hold.
