This closes the list the ledger called the work the packages turn to next. Every
Nexus module now carries a row of its own or is recorded as a shape, and nothing on
either side is marked `uncovered` or `cited, not traced`. What remains is depth:
no module on either side is accounted for in full, and the ledger says so in its own
counts rather than in a promise.

A note on verification, as for the two packages before it. The external readers
were still unavailable, so this package was checked by the gate, a reading of the
pinned source, and running the pinned records and the loader on the cases each
claim turns on. That caught two things the reading had passed: a claim that this
record carries more refusals than any other on either side, which is false —
`praxis/core/domain/events.py` carries fifty-two against its twenty-seven — and
three citations stopping on the line before the raise that does the refusing. The
loader's guard was run against both a path climbing out of its base and a link
inside the base pointing out of it, and refuses both. The articles carry status
`written`, as 278 of the 279 do.

## Nexus package 16 — the seam's remaining records

Scope: the six modules left on the Nexus side of the seam that the ledger reaches
only by citation — the translation that turns a passed decision into an
instruction, the two records a venue's own news arrives as, the sets of named
values mirroring the trading side's contract, and the queue outcomes come back
through — and what each checks and what each does not.

Three of the six make no check at all. Of the three that do, one refuses a record
that would say nothing is wrong.

A kind of command that one record checks for and nothing builds is a subject of its own, and it is the far side of the refusal above |

Six subjects promote and ten rows merge, with nothing declined.

The translation comes first, because what it refuses decides whether anything is
built at all, and the kind of command nothing builds second, as the far side of one
of those refusals. The values it stands in for an exit come third. The two records
a venue's own news arrives as follow, each with its own subject, because what each
refuses has nothing in common with the other beyond the shape of its tests. The
sets of named values come last, with the queue beside them, as the two of these six that ask
nothing of what they are given.

## Nexus package 15 — health, and the mode it settles

Scope: the three modules that decide whether an account may trade — the snapshot of
how the trading side is faring and the policy it is weighed against, the turn that
pulls one and acts on it, and the records holding the mode and the reasons it is
held there. Three modules the ledger reaches only by citation, and twenty refusals
between them.

What a stop leaves running on this loop, which of its work sits inside the lock,
the five measures a report carries, and that the report is read to restrict or halt
an account are already written. The policy itself is not: what each of the five
checking records refuses, which measures are weighed against which threshold, which
thresholds no chain weighs a measure against, and what a turn does when one of the
steps before its write fails.

### Merge and promote, package 15

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-429 | Four of a health snapshot's figures are walked by name and each refused unless it is a finite number at or above nought, with a true-or-false refused outright. | Promote. What the record of how the trading side is faring refuses is in no article, the one that has its five measures covering what they are and who reads them rather than what the record asks of them |
| Row H-430 | Two of those four are then held to a ceiling of one, by a second walk over the pair. | Merge with H-429. The two shares are the only figures with a ceiling, and the second walk is where it is put on |
| Row H-431 | The count of failures in a row is held apart from the four: a whole number, a true-or-false refused, and at or above nought. | Merge with H-429. It is the one figure of the five the first walk does not reach, so what it is held to instead belongs with what the other four are held to |
| Row H-432 | Ten thresholds are walked and each refused unless it is a finite number at or above nought, with three more walked for being whole numbers on the same terms. | Promote. What the policy refuses is a second record with its own subject, and no article reaches it |
| Row H-433 | Four orderings are each checked as one chain of two comparisons, so a middle threshold below the first or above the last is refused. | Merge with H-432. The orderings are the only checks that put two of the record's fields side by side, and they read with the rest of what it asks |

That the report can restrict or halt an account is written; which measure is weighed against which threshold, and that the answer is the worst of them rather than a sum, is in no article |
| Row H-437 | The drift from the exchange's clock has a halt threshold and no breach one, so drift either halts or counts for nothing. | Merge with H-435. It is the one measure in the first chain and not the second, which reads only against both chains |

The snapshot comes first and the policy second, because the policy is what the
snapshot is weighed against; the weighing third, and what it ignores fourth. The
turn follows, then the two roads its answer takes, then the records that hold the
answer. One subject is held out of the records: the moments with no marker, because the
records around them on this side demand one.

mode controller, and a count of three steps stated where a turn without a refresher
does two. The articles carry status `written`, which is what 265 of the 266 carry;

## Nexus package 14 — what five small records refuse

Scope: the five records left on the Nexus side with refusals in them, and the
checks each makes on itself — the event written down so a count of losses can be
rebuilt, the signal a predictor hands back, the settings a strategy is configured
with, the word that a bracket's protection was lost and dealt with, and the limits
that trip the breakers. Five modules, nineteen refusals between them, and no
article cites any of the five. The event the first of them holds is reached
all the same, through the line that builds it and the one that reads it back,
by two articles citing neither of its own checks.

Two of the five seal a copy of a mapping over the field it arrived in, by the same
line, and a third record on this side already has an article for doing the same.

### Merge and promote, package 14

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-390 | Every strategy event is refused unless the strategy's reference and the kind it names are each text with something left in them once trimmed. | Promote. What the event written down for rebuilding a count of losses is checked for is in no article, the two that reach the event citing the line that builds it and the line that reads it back rather than any of its own checks |
| Row H-391 | The result it carries must be a finite figure, and no check of the record's own weighs that figure against nought, so a decimal loss, a decimal gain and a decimal nought are all let in. | Merge with H-390. That the record itself is indifferent to the sign its reader turns on belongs with the rest of what it does ask |
| Row H-392 | Its moment must be a moment, and must carry the UTC marker itself. | Merge with H-390, the same subject read on past the reference and the kind |
| Row H-393 | The outcome's reference must be text, and empty text is allowed where text with nothing left in it once trimmed is refused. | Promote. A field whose empty value is allowed and whose blank value is refused is a subject of its own: the record has no way of saying the field is missing except by that empty text, and no article reaches it |
| Row H-394 | That field cannot be left as a none at all: empty text is what stands for its absence, and the record names that empty text as its own default. | Merge with H-393. The reason the empty text has to be allowed is that nothing else can stand for the field being missing, which is the half that makes the first worth reading |
| Row H-395 | Every signal is refused unless the predictor's reference is text with something left in it once trimmed, and unless what it was handed for its values is a dictionary. | Promote. What the signal a predictor hands back is checked for is in no article |
| Row H-396 | Every key in those values must be text. | Merge with H-395, the first of the two things asked of what the mapping holds |
| Row H-397 | A value that is a whole number or a floating one must be finite, and so must a decimal one, where a value of any other kind is weighed in no way at all. | Merge with H-395. That the checks reach only the numbers and let everything else by is the sharpest of them and reads only beside the rest |
| Row H-398 | Its moment must be a moment, and must carry the UTC marker itself. | Merge with H-395, the same subject read on past the values |
| Row H-399 | The values are copied and sealed over the field they arrived in, so a caller altering which entries its own mapping holds changes nothing the signal's mapping holds. | Promote. Three records on this side seal a mapping by the same line, and what that sealing reaches and what it leaves alone is one subject across the three; the article on the command's settings has the copying and the sealing and not what they leave alone, which package eleven's re-seed recorded as still open |

| Row H-410 | The command's settings are sealed by the same line, so the same thing holds there: a caller reaching into one of the objects its entries hold alters what the command holds. | Merge with H-399. This is the candidate package eleven's re-seed left open, and it is the same line in a third module, so it is written here rather than left standing |
| Row H-401 | A signal shipped across a process boundary is rebuilt from three parts with its values unsealed, and the rebuilding runs every one of its checks again. | Merge with H-399. The rebuilding hands the values over unsealed and the rebuilt record seals them again, so it exists to undo and redo exactly what the sealing did, and the two read together |
| Row H-402 | A parameter set is refused unless what it was handed is a dictionary, and nothing else about it is weighed. | Promote. The settings a strategy is configured with are the thinnest record on this side, asking one thing and answering one, and no article reaches them |
| Row H-404 | Asking a parameter set for something it does not hold answers with whatever the asking named as its default. | Merge with H-402, the one thing it answers beside the one thing it asks |
| Row H-405 | Four of its fields are walked by name, and each is refused unless it is text with something left in it once trimmed. | Promote. What the word that a bracket's protection was lost and dealt with is checked for is in no article |
| Row H-406 | Its moment must be a moment and must carry the UTC marker itself, both weighed by one test rather than two. | Merge with H-405, the same subject read on past the four |
| Row H-407 | The attempt number must be a whole number at or above one, and a true-or-false is refused by a test of its own set ahead of the one for whole numbers, which would otherwise count such a thing as one. | Merge with H-405. The same guard against a true-or-false standing in for a number appears on the instruction this side sends, where it reads as a section inside that record's own checks rather than as a subject; it reads the same way here, as one field of a record whose other checks are plainer |
| Row H-408 | Three limits may each be left out, and leaving one out is passed over before that limit's own figure is looked at rather than refused. | Promote. The limits that trip the breakers are in no article, and the line between a limit left out and a limit set to nought is where this record's two checks meet |
| Row H-409 | A limit that is given must be a finite figure above nought, so a limit of nought is refused rather than let in as a limit. | Merge with H-408, the far side of the same choice |

Seven subjects promote and fourteen rows merge, with nothing declined: none of the
five modules is cited by any article, and one row reaches back to a sixth module
to close a candidate an earlier package left open.

The five records are taken in the order the scope names them, and within each what
it asks comes before what it answers. Two subjects are held out of the first
record: the field whose empty value stands for its absence, because the record has
no other way of saying the field is missing. One is held out across three records
rather than inside any of them: the sealing of a mapping, which is the same line in
all three, and whose two halves — what it reaches, and what it leaves shared — only
read together. The crossing of a process boundary goes with it because what
crosses is the sealing undone and then done again.

drafted looser than the test allows: the rows and the articles for two records
were drafted saying a mapping, where the test names one concrete kind, so a
mapping that is not of that kind is refused and a kind built on it is allowed.
Both were corrected to say a dictionary before either was committed, which is why
the rows above read as they do. Both were caught by a reviewer running the pinned
record rather than reading it, which is how each was pinned down, though a close
enough reading of the checks would have shown either.

That is the third package in a row whose defects were all semantic, and the first
of the three in which no check was drafted at all, the two before it each having
drafted one and lost it in survey. Neither class here was drafted against: a type claim's looseness is not visible in the text at all, and the
first class was already surveyed and found unmatched in package 13. What has
worked instead, twice now, is a reviewer told to build the counterexample and run
the pinned class against it rather than argue from the text — a reviewing
instruction rather than a gate, and recorded here as one.

## Nexus package 13 — what a capital lock and an order's context refuse

Scope: the two records Nexus keeps around an order — the money held for it from
before it goes, and the note it needs to make sense of what
comes back — and the checks each makes on itself. Two modules, fourteen refusals
each, all but one of them standing before the record exists, and no article
reaches either.

The money held is written up elsewhere as the controller's work: what refuses a
request for it, and the walk that gives it back when it runs out. What the record
itself refuses is a separate matter and is in no article.

### Merge and promote, package 13

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-374 | Every capital lock is refused unless its own reference and the reference of the strategy that asked for it are each text with something left in them once trimmed. | Promote. What the record of money held is checked for is in no article, the articles beside it covering what refuses a request for money rather than what refuses the record |
| Row H-375 | The two sums it holds must each be a finite figure at or above nought, so a lock holding nothing at all is allowed. | Merge with H-374. That a lock may hold nothing is the sharpest of its checks and reads only beside the rest of them |
| Row H-376 | Both its moments must be moments, and each must carry the UTC marker itself. | Promote. The two moments are a subject of their own: they are the only pair the record weighs against each other |
| Row H-377 | The moment it runs out at must stand strictly after the moment it was made at, so a lock that runs out as it is made is refused. | Merge with H-376, the one check that puts two of the record's fields side by side |
| Row H-378 | The sum a lock holds altogether is its two sums added, worked out on each reading rather than kept. | Promote. A lock is asked two things after it exists, and neither is part of building it: this one adds two of its fields together and nothing checks the answer |
| Row H-379 | Asking whether a lock has run out refuses a moment that does not carry the UTC marker, and answers yes at the moment it runs out as well as past it. | Merge with H-378, the lock's second question. It refuses a moment the lock does not hold, where every other refusal of the lock weighs a field the lock itself holds, so it reads beside the other thing asked after the fact |
| Row H-380 | A record of an attempt is held to four checks: a granted one must carry a lock and must not carry a reason, and a refused one must carry a reason and must not carry a lock. | Promote. The record of the attempt is a second record in the same module, and what it refuses is in no article |
| Row H-381 | No check in the module asks whether the yes-or-no on that record is a yes-or-no, where the record beside it checks the type of every field it has. | Merge with H-380. The record of the attempt asks the type of none of its three fields, where the record beside it asks the type of all six of its own, and the field all four of its checks turn on is the one it reads as a yes-or-no without asking whether it is one |
| Row H-382 | Every context for an outcome is refused unless its command's reference and the strategy's are each text with something left in them once trimmed, with a trade's reference held to the same where one is given. | Promote. What the note Nexus needs to read an outcome is checked for is in no article |
| Row H-383 | Its side must be one of the named ones, and both its yes-or-no fields must be yes-or-no. | Merge with H-382, the same subject read on past the three references |
| Row H-386 | Its worth and the fees expected with it must each be a finite figure, and the worth must stand above nought where the fees need only be at or above it. | Merge with H-382. The two figures it always carries belong with the rest of what it always answers for |
| Row H-384 | A size may be left out only where the order grows a position: an order that reduces one and names no size is refused. | Promote. The size is the only field of the note held to one rule when it is there and another when it is not, and no article reaches either |
| Row H-385 | A size that is given must be a finite figure above nought. | Merge with H-384. The two stand in the two arms of one choice on whether a size was given at all, so what is asked of a size that is there belongs with what is asked when it is not |
| Row H-389 | The flag saying the order grows a position is never weighed against the side, each being checked on its own, so a buy may be marked as reducing and a sell as growing. | Promote. The flag that routes a fill is in no article, and the check the record does not make on it is the reason it has to be supplied from outside |
| Row H-388 | Whether the order reduces a position is the growing flag turned round, worked out on each reading rather than kept. | Merge with H-389, the second way of asking the one flag |
| Row H-387 | The flag saying an exit was meant to close the trade outright is refused on an order that grows a position. | Merge with H-389. It is the one other field the growing flag governs, and it is governed the opposite way round from the size: a size must be there where the order reduces a position, and this flag must stand false where the order grows one |

Seven subjects promote and nine rows merge, with nothing declined: both modules
were untouched, so there was nothing to collide with, and every row traced for
them was new.

The money held comes first and the note second, because the lock is taken before
the order goes and the note is read after it comes back. Within each, what the
record always answers for comes before the fields it may leave out. Three
subjects are held out of the money held: the two moments, because they are the
only pair it compares; the two things it is asked after it exists, because neither
is part of building it; and the record of the attempt, because it is a second
record with its own four checks. Two are held out of the note: the size,
because it is the one field held to one rule when it is there and another when it
is not, and the flag saying the order grows a position, because nothing
ever asks whether it agrees with the side.

| ID | Topic | Status | Article |
|---|---|---|---|
| N13-01 | What money held for an order is checked for | written | [what-money-held-for-an-order-is-checked-for.md](atoms/what-money-held-for-an-order-is-checked-for.md) |
| N13-02 | The two moments a hold carries | written | [the-two-moments-a-hold-carries.md](atoms/the-two-moments-a-hold-carries.md) |
| N13-07 | What a hold is asked after it exists | written | [what-a-hold-is-asked-after-it-exists.md](atoms/what-a-hold-is-asked-after-it-exists.md) |

| N13-04 | What a note for an outcome is checked for | written | [what-a-note-for-an-outcome-is-checked-for.md](atoms/what-a-note-for-an-outcome-is-checked-for.md) |
| N13-05 | The size a note may leave out | written | [the-size-a-note-may-leave-out.md](atoms/the-size-a-note-may-leave-out.md) |
| N13-06 | The flag saying an order grows a position | written | [the-flag-saying-an-order-grows-a-position.md](atoms/the-flag-saying-an-order-grows-a-position.md) |

### Re-seed, after writing package 13

What six rounds of verifying the seven articles turned up that none of them
reaches. Each was checked against the index and the ledger before it was written
down here. Paths are under `nexus/` except where named otherwise.

| Candidate | What proves it |
|---|---|
| The way of asking whether a lock has run out that nothing calls | `nexus/core/capital_controller/reservation.py` 92 defines a way of asking whether a lock has run out, and the only calls to it in either repository are the five in `tests/test_reservation.py` at 62, 66, 70, 174 and 180. It answers yes at the expiry moment itself, at 103. The running system asks the question twice without it, and the two draw the line differently: `what-becomes-of-money-set-aside.md` already has that difference, citing `nexus/core/capital_controller/capital_controller.py` 480 against 563. That the record carries a third answer to the same question, agreeing with the sweep and not with the claim, and called by neither, is in no article |
| The road that leaves the note's second flag at its default | Four roads in the two systems' own code build a note, with tests building it too. Three supply its second yes-or-no — `praxis/launcher.py` 1531 from the decision's own context, 1587 fixed true, and 1648 from the event it rebuilds from — and the fourth, `nexus/startup/shutdown_sequencer.py` 518-527, names it nowhere, so it takes the false at `nexus/infrastructure/praxis_connector/order_context.py` 68. `what-praxis-works-out-for-an-exit.md` already has the fixed-true road, citing 1587. That the road on the Nexus side never marks a full close at all is in no article |
| The worth the note is given where the command for the same exit is given nought | `nexus/startup/shutdown_sequencer.py` 508 works out a worth from the position's entry price and the size asked for, and 524 puts that on the note, where 564 puts nought on the context the command is built from and `nexus/infrastructure/praxis_connector/translate.py` 120 copies that nought into the command. So one exit is described by two records carrying different worths. `the-sale-a-host-sends-as-it-stops.md` already has the command's nought, citing 564-565, and already has the building refused for a worth at or below nought, citing 499-516; that the note is given a worth at all on that road, and that it is the entry price times the size, is in neither it nor any other article |

Three candidates. All three are about a road rather than a record: the records were
read end to end for this package, and what reads them or fills them in was not.
That is the shape of what a package on a record leaves behind.

Verifying this package found one class of defect seven times, in four articles and
twice over in the index and the changelog: a sentence that is true of one check
and stated of the whole record. A check refusing a figure below nought and not one
at it does not make a record holding nothing acceptable; an absent size clearing
the test that allows it does not make the note valid. Each instance read as a
harmless summary of the paragraph above it, and each was false by a
counterexample the reviewer could build and run. The fix in every case was to
name the check the sentence is about.

A blocking check was drafted for that class and measured against the clean corpus
first, as the rule requires, and it was not written. Refusing an acceptance verb —
passes, is allowed, clears, goes through — in a page's prose fires on 57 of the
245 pages, and nearly all of those are sound: one article's subject is the checks
a decision passes, several use the word transitively, and others name the check in
the same sentence. The class is semantic, as the one package 12 found was. What the two
surveys show is narrower than a limit: a check matching the verb alone cannot
separate these, and the cases it fires on wrongly are distinguishable — several
name the check in the same sentence — so a narrower check may yet be worth
measuring. Neither survey found one, and until one is measured the class is caught
by reading.

The second of the two reviewers went offline partway through this package, its
account out of credit. Of the thirteen defects found in package 12 it had found
nine, including every instance of prose outrunning its citations. Running the one
remaining reviewer twice, the second time under an adversarial brief naming the
class to hunt, recovered some of that: on the merge it returned nothing and then
found three real defects on the second pass, and on the articles it returned
nothing and then found three more. The adversarial brief also over-corrected once,
returning eleven findings of which eight demanded that every field's plain-English
name be derivable from a check — a standard the whole corpus is written against,
and one the committed and twice-reviewed article on what a command is checked for
would fail. Two readers disagreeing is worth more than one reader pushed harder.

## Nexus package 12 — what an outcome refuses

Scope: the record Praxis hands back for an instruction it worked through, on the
roads that build one, and the checks it makes on itself before it exists — what every one of them is
refused for, what a fill must carry and a non-fill must not, which kinds may
carry a reason and which must not, and the two measures of how an execution went
that mean one thing absent and another at nought. One module, the counterpart to
the record going the other way, and no article reaches it.

What every outcome is checked for is split either side of the choice between the
two lists. Two names, a kind, a moment and the two measures are checked before
it; a reason and the size still working are checked after it.

### Merge and promote, package 12

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-363 | Every outcome is refused unless its own reference and the reference of the command it answers are each text with something left in them once trimmed. | Promote. What an outcome is checked for whatever its kind is in no article, and it is what the two lists are reached on top of |
| Row H-364 | Its kind must be one of the named ones, its moment must be a moment, and that moment must carry the UTC marker itself. | Merge with H-363, the same subject read on past the two references |
| Row H-365 | A size left over, where one is given at all, must be a finite figure at or above nought. | Merge with H-363. It is checked after the choice between the two lists rather than before it, and it is still asked of every kind, so it belongs with the rest of what every outcome answers |
| Row H-366 | One test on the kind sends an outcome to the list for a fill or the list for anything else, so it is held to one of the two and never to both. | Promote. What a fill must carry and anything else must not is the subject the scope puts second, and no article reaches it |
| Row H-367 | A fill must carry four figures: a size, a price, an amount, and the fees paid. | Merge with H-366, the near side of the same choice |
| Row H-368 | Each of the four must be a finite figure, and the first three must stand above nought where the fees need only be at or above it. | Merge with H-366. That the fees alone may be nought is the sharpest of the four and the one a reader would otherwise get wrong |
| Row H-369 | An outcome carrying no fill must carry none of those four. | Merge with H-366, the far side of the same choice |
| Row H-370 | A refusal must carry a reason with something left in it once trimmed, and no other kind may carry a refusal's reason. | Promote. Which kinds may carry a reason is in no article, and the two reasons are held to different rules that only read together |
| Row H-371 | A cancellation may carry a reason or leave it out, but not one with nothing left in it once trimmed, and no other kind may carry a cancellation's reason. | Merge with H-370. One reason is required and the other optional, and each is refused on every kind but its own, so the pair is one subject |
| Row H-372 | Two measures of how an execution went may each be left out, and leaving one out is passed over rather than refused. | Promote. The two measures are in no article, and what they mean absent and at nought is the one place this record carries a figure it does not weigh against anything |
| Row H-373 | A measure given on an outcome carrying no fill is refused, or else a measure given on an outcome carrying a fill must be a finite figure. | Merge with H-372, what a measure must be once it is there |

Four subjects promote and seven rows merge, with nothing declined: the module no
article reaches leaves nothing to collide with, and every row traced for it was
new.

What an outcome is checked for whatever its kind comes first, and the two lists
second, because the choice between them is made partway through those checks. The
size left over is checked last of all and could have gone with the kind's own
list; it stays with the first subject because it is asked of every kind, which is
the thing that subject is. The reasons and the measures are each held out because
each turns on a distinction the first two cannot carry — one kind's reason
required where another's is optional, and a figure whose absence says something
other than nought.


| ID | Topic | Status | Article |
|---|---|---|---|
| N12-01 | What an outcome is checked for | written | [what-an-outcome-is-checked-for.md](atoms/what-an-outcome-is-checked-for.md) |
| N12-02 | What a fill must carry | written | [what-a-fill-must-carry.md](atoms/what-a-fill-must-carry.md) |
| N12-03 | The reason an outcome may carry | written | [the-reason-an-outcome-may-carry.md](atoms/the-reason-an-outcome-may-carry.md) |
| N12-04 | The two measures an outcome reports | written | [the-two-measures-an-outcome-reports.md](atoms/the-two-measures-an-outcome-reports.md) |

### Re-seed, after writing package 12

What six rounds of verifying the four articles turned up that none of them
reaches. Each was checked against the index and the ledger before it was written
down here. Paths are under `nexus/` except where named otherwise.

| Candidate | What proves it |
|---|---|
| What an outcome's figures are never weighed against each other | `nexus/infrastructure/praxis_connector/trade_outcome.py` 92-148, 166-178, 180-235 and 240-254 hold every check the record makes, and no line in any of them compares two of the record's own fields. So a fill may report a size, a price and an amount that do not multiply out, and a size still working that stands above the size filled. N12-01 and N12-02 each say what one figure must be on its own; that no two are ever put side by side is in no article, and the same subject on the Praxis side is a different module |
| The six kinds of outcome, and the two cuts across them that overlap in one | `nexus/infrastructure/praxis_connector/trade_outcome_type.py` 26-31 names six, 37-42 answers for four of them and 48 for two, so one kind answers to both cuts and one answers to neither. The second cut is the test N12-02's whole choice turns on. The ledger marks the module reached by N1-07 and N7-05, and each of those cites it in passing for one group; the six and the two cuts are in no article |

Two candidates. The first is the shape the Praxis side already has an article for,
found absent on this side only because the module was read end to end for the
figures rather than for what joins them. The second is a module the ledger has
shown reached since the first package, by two articles that each needed one of its
groupings in passing, which is how a module can be marked reached and still have
nothing said about it.

Verifying this package found the same defect twice, in two different articles, and
then a third time in a package already committed: a lead sentence that places the
subject in the system — who hands the record over, and when the checks run —
where the article's own claims establish neither, both coming from the module's
docstring. The sections were sound in every case. A lead is read as scene-setting
rather than as a claim, by the writer and the reviewer both, which is what lets an
unsourced sentence sit in the one place every reader starts. The eleventh
package's lead was corrected for it.

A blocking check was drafted for that class and measured against the clean corpus
first, as the rule requires, and it was not written. Refusing a verb of handing
over in a lead fires on 17 of the 238 pages, nearly all of them pages where the
handing over is what the subject is. Narrowing it to pages whose claims cite a
single source file leaves four to inspect and misses the known defective lead,
whose claims cite two. The class is semantic and the check would have to read
whether a sentence stands on a claim, which the gate cannot do.

## Nexus package 11 — what a command refuses

Scope: the record Nexus hands Praxis on the roads that build one, and the checks
it makes on itself before it exists at all — what every one of them is refused for
whatever it asks, what each of the three kinds must carry and must not, and the one
place where the record alters what it was handed rather than refusing it.
One module, the largest block of refusals left anywhere on either side, and no
article reaches it.

What every command is checked for comes first, because the checks each kind makes
on top of those assume them.

### Merge and promote, package 11

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-354 | Every command is refused unless its reference, its account, its venue and its symbol are each text with something left in them once trimmed. | Merge with H-357. The four that must be there and the thirteen that need not are one subject read from either end: what a command is checked for whatever it asks |
| Row H-355 | Its kind must be one of the three named ones, and the amount it names must be a finite figure at or above nought. | Merge with H-357, the same |
| Row H-356 | Its moment must be a moment, and must carry the UTC marker itself: a zone that merely stands at UTC is refused, where the same connector accepts one on its other two handoffs. | Promote. The disagreement between one record and the two handoffs beside it is a subject of its own, and no article reaches either side of it |
| Row H-357 | Thirteen things a command may leave out are each refused where present and wrong, each test standing behind one for its absence. | Promote. What a command is checked for before its kind is weighed is in no article, and it is what the checks each kind makes on top of it assume |
| Row H-358 | A deadline given as a true-or-false is refused outright, though such a thing counts as a whole number. | Merge with H-357, the sharpest of the thirteen and the one a reader would otherwise get wrong |
| Row H-359 | A set of settings is not refused for being handed in as something that can still be altered: it is copied and sealed in place of what arrived. | Promote. A sealed record that rewrites one of its own fields rather than refusing it is in no article, and it is the one place this one does anything but check |
| Row H-360 | A new order must carry a side, a self-trade setting, and exactly one of a size or a spend. | Promote. What each kind must and must not carry is the subject the scope puts second, and no article reaches it |
| Row H-361 | A change must carry none of the eleven things beyond the common ones, each refused on its own. | Merge with H-360, the far side of the same subject |
| Row H-362 | A cancel must carry none of those eleven either, refused the same way. | Merge with H-360. That the two kinds refuse the same eleven separately, rather than sharing one check, belongs with what each kind requires |

Four subjects promote and five rows merge, with nothing declined: the module no
article reaches leaves nothing to collide with, and every row traced for it was
new.

What a command is checked for comes first, and what each kind must carry second,
because the second runs only once the first has passed. The moment's marker and
the settings that are rewritten both sit inside the first and could have folded
into it; each is held out because what makes it worth reading is a comparison the
first cannot carry — one against the two handoffs beside this record, the other
against every other check in the module, which refuses rather than rewrites.

| N11-02 | The marker a command's moment must carry | written | [the-marker-a-commands-moment-must-carry.md](atoms/the-marker-a-commands-moment-must-carry.md) |
| N11-03 | The settings a command rewrites | written | [the-settings-a-command-rewrites.md](atoms/the-settings-a-command-rewrites.md) |
| N11-04 | What each kind of command must carry | written | [what-each-kind-of-command-must-carry.md](atoms/what-each-kind-of-command-must-carry.md) |

## Nexus package 10 — how a strategy is reached, and what it is handed

Scope: the one wrapper each strategy sits behind and the lock it holds, the eight
ways in and the single line that reaches each of them, what a strategy must
provide and what it may leave alone, the timer road and what a turn of it
abandons or schedules, and what a strategy is handed on the roads that hand it
anything at all. Five modules no article reaches at all, which is the largest block of
untouched Nexus behaviour left and the reason this package was taken before the
four candidates the Praxis side's sixth package left re-seeded.

The wrapper and its lock comes first because it is the one thing every dispatch
passes through, and nothing in the wiki yet says a strategy is wrapped at all.
Two of the eight ways in run before the first timer is registered or the first
thing a strategy is handed is built, which is a finding of its own.

### Merge and promote, package 10

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-338 | Each strategy sits behind a wrapper holding a lock of its own, and every one of the eight ways in takes that lock before the strategy sees anything. | Promote. Nothing in the wiki says a strategy is wrapped at all, and the lock that wrapper holds is taken on every one of the eight ways in |
| Row H-339 | Because that lock is made one per wrapper, two strategies' callbacks can run at the same moment where one strategy's cannot. | Merge with H-338, which is what having one lock per wrapper rather than one for all of them costs |
| Row H-340 | Outside the tests, each of the eight ways in is reached from exactly one line, and nothing under `praxis/` reaches any of them. | Promote. Five of the eight roads are already named as roads that reach a strategy — N7-06 the signal, N7-07 the load, N6-02 the stop, the article on what a stopping host saves of its strategies the save, and three articles the outcome — which leaves the start, the timer and the event replay named nowhere; and that each of the eight is reached from exactly one line outside the tests, with nothing under `praxis/` reaching any of them, is in none of them |
| Row H-341 | A strategy must provide seven callbacks and may leave the eighth alone, the replay one carrying a body that does nothing with what it is handed. | Promote. What a strategy has to supply is the first thing anyone writing one asks, and no article says |
| Row H-342 | The thing that routes to a strategy refuses three things at the door: a name that is not a string, one that is empty once trimmed, and one it holds no wrapper for. | Promote. The refusals are what a caller meets, and they are in no article |
| Row H-343 | It is built refusing five more: a mapping that is not one, a key that is not a usable name, a value that is not a wrapper, a wrapper whose own name disagrees with its key, and two keys that trim to one name. | Merge with H-342. The same subject read at the other end: what the router will not be built from, against what it will not be asked |
| Row H-344 | Every way in trims the name before the lookup, so a name given with spaces round it reaches the same wrapper as one without. | Merge with H-342, which is why the second of its three refusals is about a name empty once trimmed rather than empty as given |
| Row H-345 | Each timer is its own one-shot countdown, marked so it cannot hold the process up, and kept under a name made of the strategy and the timer joined by a nul. | Merge with H-346 as what a turn is scheduled by |
| Row H-346 | A turn tests twice whether the loop still runs, once before it asks for the context and once after, so a stop landing between the two abandons the turn with the context already fetched. | Promote. What a turn of a timer does, and gives up on, is in no article; N7-06 has the loop that drives predictions and not this one |
| Row H-347 | Of the five roads that hand a strategy any settings at all, every one builds them empty on the spot, the manifest figures going onto the thing it is handed instead. | Merge with H-351. What a strategy is handed is the subject, and that the settings beside it are always empty belongs there; N6-02 has the stopping road's half of it |
| Row H-348 | A turn whose work raises is logged and passed over and the next turn still asked for, but the asking stands outside the catch, and where the loop has stopped it builds no timer. | Merge with H-346. A raise in the work is caught and the next turn still asked for, so this is the road that carries on where H-349's is the one that stops |
| Row H-349 | Where either running test sends the turn back, no next turn is scheduled, so a stop ends the chain where a failure does not. | Merge with H-346. Its two returns end a turn already under way, and the contrast with H-348, which carries on instead, is what the article turns on |
| Row H-350 | A submitter that raises is caught around the call alone, which logs and goes on to ask for the next turn, so nothing retries the actions and how far the submitter got is left where it fell. | Merge with H-346. The inner of the road's two catches, and the one that loses work rather than ending anything |
| Row H-351 | What a strategy is handed carries three things and refuses five: holdings that are not a sealed run, anything in that run that is not a holding, an amount that is not a decimal, an amount that is not finite or falls below nought, and a mode that is not one of the named ones. | Promote. N6-02 has what the stopping road puts on it; what it is and what it refuses is nowhere |
| Row H-352 | The thing handed over cannot be altered while each holding inside it can. | Merge with H-351. Sealing the thing handed over stops its own fields being rebound and does nothing to the holdings inside it, which are not sealed, so a field on a holding can be assigned either way |
| Row H-353 | The note at the top of what a strategy is handed says a holding a strategy alters is ignored, and nothing ignores it: the holdings put on it are the host's own objects. | Decline. N6-02 states what the note denies: the holdings are the host's own, so altering one alters what the host holds. The note itself is kept here as stale, beside H-040 and H-058 |

Six subjects promote, nine rows merge and one declines, and the balance owes more
to coverage than to anything about the subjects: five modules no article reaches
leave far less to collide with than the two packages before this one met. Less,
not nothing. Three rows of this table meet N6-02 — the settings the stopping road
hands over, what that road puts on the thing a strategy is handed, and the note
about altering a holding — and the one decline is to N6-02 as well.

That note is worth saying plainly. N6-02 has the holdings a stopping strategy is
handed being the host's own objects, so altering one alters what the host holds.
The note at the top of the file says such an alteration is ignored, and nothing
ignores it. The register keeps that as a stale note rather than an article,
beside H-040 and H-058.

The wrapper comes first because every road passes through it: each of the eight
ways in takes the wrapper's lock before the strategy sees anything. The roads
come next, being the eight that do the passing. What a strategy must supply and
what the router refuses follow, both standing before any road arrives — the one a
contract on the strategy itself, the other a lookup every dispatch runs before it
reaches the wrapper at all. The timer road and what a strategy is handed come
last, and can be read in either order.

Two candidates, and both are the far half of a fact an article already carries

true-or-false test is written and which of the pair it catches is not. In both
the evidence was in front of the writing — N11-03 already cites the line that
proves the first — and in both the article stopped at the half its claims stated.

Two further findings were written down here and struck out. That a record of this
name on the Praxis side is checked differently is already written, with its four
required names and its zone-and-offset rule, and the module carrying it is marked
covered in full. That a new order is free to carry the seven things a change and
a cancel are refused does not hold as a freedom: the checks every command makes
still apply to it, what sends an entry requires three of the seven before a
command is built at all, and the record's own notes say which are populated for
which kind. Neither survived its first reading.

Six candidates: four about what a strategy is handed, and two about the timer
road. The four are one gap from different sides. Five of the eight roads hand a
strategy one of these things, built in three places that do not agree: one of the
three hands no holdings whatever is held, and the three take the mode from three
different readings. Of the other three roads, two hand a
payload and nothing else, and one hands nothing at all.

N10-06 has what that thing is, what it refuses, and that four of the roads build
the settings beside it empty — the last of those folded in at the Merge. What
verifying it turned up sits beside that rather than inside it: not what the thing
is, but how unalike the roads are that build one. That is where the packages go
next.

## Nexus package 9 — which hosts checkpoint, and the roads into the log nothing takes

Scope: which hosts run the checkpoint timer and which take only the one a start
may make, what a failed checkpoint costs a start, which clock the log keeps,
what the cut's moving cutoff costs, which outcomes ask for no write of the
name they record, and the two roads into the log that nothing in either package
drives. The five candidates the package-8 re-seed named, taken through the
stages together. Which hosts run the timer is the subject that must come before
the rest: N7-08 names a timer among the three that ask for a checkpoint and
N8-05 says what a turn of one does, and neither says which hosts have one
running, so a reader of either is left to take every host for the live one.

Four rows promote: which host checkpoints, which clock a record is stamped off,
the road that empties the log, and the name a success records. Which host
checkpoints comes first, as the scope put it, because a reader of the timer's
article or the checkpoint's is left taking every host for the live one. What a
failed checkpoint costs a start goes with it rather than standing alone: the
road out of the sequence is short, and ends in two articles already written.
Were that article to outgrow the cap in the writing, the failure is the half
that leaves. The clock and the emptying road are the log's own two. The name a
success records comes last, and is the valuation loop's question asked of a
different part: a change to the state that reaches the log only where something
else writes.

One more article came out of the writing. Which host checkpoints outgrew the cap
by two words, so the failure left as the half named above, giving N9-05. Five
articles, then, where the merge planned four.

The moving cutoff nearly made a sixth. N7-09 stood one word under the cap when
it was added, so the moments the cut compares left as an article of their own;
then the rounds took back from that article everything its neighbours already
held — the comparison against the record's stamp to N8-04 and N7-09, where the
stamp comes from to N9-02 —
until one fact was left. N7-09 had room for one fact, the duplicated material
having gone from it too, so the cutoff went back where this table put it.

The writing narrowed one of the four as well. The name a success records was put
as covering the recording and the two roads that reach a success with no mark on
it, and both of those turned out to be N1-06's: it has the recording, and names
both the acknowledgement that moves no money and the exit whose failed
giving-back is passed over. What was left is whether anything writes that name,
which is the question this table had already framed, so the article is that,
naming the two roads only to show such a success is reachable and sending the
reader to N1-06 for them. Where the row had the name going unwritten outright,
the writing found it riding out on the next write of the state whatever asked
for that; and where the row had the dropping left with nothing to match, the
writing found that dropping reading the set in memory, so what the wait costs is
a restart with no write of any kind behind it.

Six rows go into articles already written rather than into new ones, which is
what a ninth package on this side looks like. A position taken in from the host
rides the start's test, which N7-08 has, along with the two changes to a
position already held that ride it. The cutoff the cut measures back from is
read off the clock as the checkpoint runs, so it moves, which N7-09 now says
beside the span it already had. What turning a payload into a record can fail at
runs to six places and one catch, where N8-08's first section was that step
naming two of them, and now names all six. And the removal in the account
thread's handler reaches nothing where the build it wraps is what failed, which
belongs beside P4-20's account of that handler. None of the four is a subject of
its own, and each sits in an article whose subject it already is.

Two rows are excluded. What the start's checkpoint turns on is N7-08's, on the
same test and the same call. What an account's thread does with such a failure
is P4-20's, which has the catch, the flag it sets and the wait every other
account is on, and puts it more exactly than the row did: one failing asks the
rest down rather than bringing them down, an account still building reaching
that wait only once it has finished building.

Four rows were raised while merging, as H-266 was in package 8. An
acknowledgement of an order that is not an entry answers with a success carrying
neither mark, which is the plain road to a name recorded where no write of it is
asked for. The other three followed a failed start checkpoint out of the
sequence to where it is caught, which is not where the sequence leaves it.

The three rows the package-8 re-seed carried are all spent. The road that
empties the file and the moment the cut reads were carried out of the rewrite's
article for answering something other than how it is written; the first becomes
an article, and the second goes into the article that owns the cut. What a
position mark is worth is spent on one thing only, why the reject and cancel
roads need a position no longer held before they answer with neither mark, and
what the other two marks are worth is unanswered still.

## Nexus package 8 — the log, and the figures that reach it

Scope: how a record reaches the log and what a damaged one costs, which of a
host's changes reach it and which wait for something else to write, the cap on
how fast entries may go in and the window that turns a repeated one away, and
the timer that checkpoints while a host runs. The five candidates the package-7
re-seed named, taken through the stages together. The log comes first: N7-08 names
the records of a state change and N7-09 the strategy events, and both say a
start reads them back; neither says how a record reaches the file, what shape
it takes there, or what a damaged one costs.

N6-11's "clear theirs and return" sits under what sets the health stop apart and contrasts the waiting, the three stops it covers being the same four lines, so it is not an account of what this one writes |
| Row H-261 | The cap on entries counts them in one list for the whole account, with nothing naming a strategy in what it holds, so every strategy on the account spends the one allowance. | Promote. N2-03 and N4-07 both cite ranges inside the reference-integrity hook and cover branches of that one hook; nothing covers this one, and the sharing is the finding |
| Row H-262 | It reads an entry alone, drops what has aged out of the second behind it, refuses where the count stands at the cap, and counts the rest. | Merge with H-261, the pass itself |
| Row H-263 | It is put in front of the other two only where a figure is carried, which on the one road a running host builds them by is the figure Praxis read from its environment and set on the settings. | Merge with H-261: whether the cap is there at all comes before what it does |
| Row H-264 | The window keeps what it has seen per strategy and identifier, drops what has aged out, and refuses one seen inside it. | Promote. The hook standing next after the rate cap, covered by no article, and working off a figure Praxis sets from a constant rather than from its environment |
| Row H-265 | Its two guards, against an entry naming no side and one naming no identifier, are never reached from a live request, the builder supplying both before either branch that makes one. | Merge with H-264: two refusals no request can reach belong with the hook that carries them |
| Row H-266 | On the two roads that clear a pending exit, the position mark is set wherever the position is found at all, the figure behind it lowered to no less than nothing, so it is set as readily where that figure already stood at nothing. | Candidate, carried. Raised by narrowing H-250, whose gloss of that mark as a position having moved was the field's own description and not what the body does. What each mark is worth is its own question, and none of this package's seven answers it |

Seven rows promote: how a record reaches the log, what a damaged one costs,
which of a host's changes reach it, how the checkpoint rewrites it and what
that costs, the timer that checkpoints while a host runs, the cap on how fast
entries may go in, and the window that turns a repeated one away. The log's own two subjects
come first, and the three after them take what reaches it, what rewrites it and
what sets the rewriting going. The cap and the window come last and stand apart:
each refuses an entry as it arrives, and neither writes anything to the log.

What a damaged record costs outgrew the cap in the writing, once the step that turns a
payload into a record was traced and found unwrapped. That fault raises where the five
before it stop quietly, so it left with the second reader that raises too, giving N8-08.
One row is excluded: what a failed write costs after an outcome is P2-41's
subject, on the same flag and the same gate. The operator road's failure is not,
and stays with the causes. Three rows are carried instead of merged: where the
cutoff is taken from, and the road that empties the log outright, both of which
sat in the rewrite's article while answering something other than how it is
written; and what a position mark is worth, which narrowing H-250 raised and
which none of the seven answers.

## Nexus package 7 — the last of stopping, and what sits either side of the checks

Scope: what a stopping host saves of its strategies and how, the last record it
writes and the account it gives back, what the walk's first stage weighs before
it reads the mode, what Praxis builds for a strategy's exit before the checks
see it, the watch over commands whose handover timed out, and what the
prediction loop's stop leaves running. The five candidates the package-6
re-seed named, taken through the stages together. The saving outgrew the cap in
the writing, giving N7-07 and later N7-12; the last record
outgrew it too, giving N7-08 and then N7-09, the
first stage giving N7-10 and N7-11, and the saving giving N7-12.

Six rows promote: what a stopping host saves of its strategies, the last record
it writes and the account it gives back, what the walk's first stage weighs
before it reads the mode, what Praxis works out for an exit before the checks
see it, the watch over commands whose handover timed out, and what the
prediction loop's stop leaves running. The first of those outgrew the cap as the
reading side was made precise, and what a start does with the saved state is
N7-07; the second outgrew it as the checkpoint itself was traced, and what a
checkpoint holds is N7-08, which outgrew it in turn as the log cut was traced,
giving N7-09; and what the first stage weighs outgrew it as each test was traced
upstream, giving N7-10 and then N7-11.

| Candidate | What proves it |
|---|---|

P6-04 has the ten roads and the guard; that those eleven are every acquisition there is — the accessor's other callers, at Praxis's `tests/test_launcher_snapshot_lock_wiring.py` 44 and Nexus's `tests/test_shutdown_sequencer.py` 646, take nothing at those lines, the second arriving at the guard already counted when 663 runs the stopping checkpoint — is in neither it nor P5-06 |
| The money's figures read without the money's lock | `nexus/core/capital_controller/capital_controller.py` 459 reads the pool with no acquisition anywhere on its road, against the ten roads that take the lock at 181, 326, 511, 546, 610, 663, 768, 875, 992 and 1055. The article citing 459-462 takes those lines for what a request's budget is and says nothing of what guards the read; P6-04 has what the lock costs a write and not what goes without it |
| Which lock the event write's caller is holding | `nexus/infrastructure/praxis_connector/outcome_processor.py` 268 holds a lock across 285, the object read at `nexus/core/domain/risk_state.py` 302, set at `praxis/launcher.py` 4128, and refused at 2569-2576 unless it is the same one 2579 puts in the pair as its first half. P5-07 has that check and N3-03 has the caller holding a lock across the write; that the lock held is the pair's own first half is in neither, as row H-329 above says |

Four candidates, and three of them are the same subject from different sides:
which roads take a lock, which take none at all, and which hold one while
another road is part way through a write. The fourth asks only which lock a hold
already written about turns out to be. The package set out to write what one
road leaves standing for another, and what verifying it turned up sits a step
below that: not what a road leaves behind, but what the locks around it do and
do not cover. That is where the packages go next.

What this package also showed is worth keeping separate from its candidates. Of
four subjects the Merge promoted, one carries no article: nothing citable was
left in it once the written claims were taken out, so there was no page to write.
The Merge had read "no article states this" claim by claim, which is the right
question for a row and the wrong one for a page, and it never asked whether what
remained was still a subject. That question was settled at the writing instead,
and is recorded above the index.

A second subject nearly went the same way, on worse ground. A reading partway
through the writing took one of its claims for written when no article held it,
and the page was cut to the one claim left. The Merge had that row right and the
reading overturned it. Both claims are back, and the correction is recorded above
the index as well. Two of the candidates above were struck on the same reasoning
and then restored, once the articles named were read to the end rather than
taken at their dispositions. The lesson is not the Merge's: a claim called
written needs the article naming it read through, and a stage that overturns an
earlier stage carries the heavier burden.

down here. Paths are under `praxis/` except where named otherwise.

| Candidate | What proves it |
|---|---|

A command the copy does not hold is never reached by the walk, which goes over that copy alone at 4459-4460 and tests only what it holds at 4461. P5-01 has the copy, the walk, and a command re-marked after the copy keeping its mark on the number; a command absent from the copy altogether is in neither it nor N8-03 |
| The two lock histories behind one write | `nexus/infrastructure/praxis_connector/outcome_processor.py` 244 reaches `nexus/core/capital_controller/capital_controller.py` 768 and leaves it on the return at 808, all before 285. Where the basis stands at nought, 422-423 hands back nothing instead and the test at 243 skips 244; a fill no larger than the position then reaches 285 with that lock never taken, where a larger one raises at 516-521 before it. So one write runs with two histories behind it and a third road never arrives. P5-07 has which locks the write itself enters; no article has either history |
| The reserve a store write waits on | `nexus/core/capital_controller/capital_controller.py` 326 takes the lock made at 107, which 127 hands `launcher.py` 2580 as the pair's second half, on a road that asks for no store write at all. Once the pair is attached, a state write takes that same half itself at `nexus/infrastructure/state_store.py` 221 under the test at 219, before it reaches 179 or 199, so a reservation under way holds that write out until it is done. P5-06 and P5-07 have what the store's writes take and when the pair arrives; what else takes it, and what that costs a write, is in neither |

That turns on the result having succeeded, 4404 requiring it, and on no other command's write having dropped the mark at 4461-4462 first. P5-01 has the write that drops no mark and N8-03 has the mark that asks for a write; that the two together can ask for a second write is in neither |

Four candidates, and they fall into two kinds. Two are a lock or a mark one road
holds while another reads it: the reserve that holds half the store's pair, and
the mark an operator's write leaves for the next outcome to act on. The other two
are gaps inside one road's own handling — a mark outside the copy that walks the
marks, and a write reached with two different lock histories behind it.

The package set out to write the Praxis half of the seam, and the seven articles
now carry it. What verifying them turned up is narrower and harder than which
road does what: what one road leaves standing or holds for another to wait on,
and what a single road's own branches leave differently behind them. That is
where the packages go next.

## Nexus package 6 — stopping a Nexus host

Scope: what a stopping host halts before it asks anything, what a strategy is
handed when it is asked to stop, the abort that travels with the sales, what
the host waits for once it has sent, what it does with an outcome it was not
waiting on, and the name an order comes back with. The four candidates the
package-5 re-seed carried, with the halting and the wait taken off the queue the
package-1 Seed left under stopping Nexus strategies.

### Merge and promote, package 6

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-191 | A host stopping hands each strategy its own positions and what is left of its slice of the pool. | Promote. What the asking carries is unanswered |
| Row H-192 | It hands the operational mode with them, and settings carrying nothing. | Merge with H-191, the rest of the same answer |
| Row H-193 | It keeps the answer only where the strategy answers with something, and a strategy that raises is logged. | Merge with H-191: what becomes of the answer |

| Row H-195 | An abort with no identifier cannot be built, so the send's guard against a missing one is never reached; a send that raises is logged. | Merge with H-194, which it is the failing of |

| Row H-198 | What is still outstanding is aborted under a different reason, waited on again for half as long, then logged and left. | Merge with H-197, the second half of the same wait |
| Row H-199 | An outcome for an identifier the host is not waiting on is handed to a handler, or logged as dropped where none is wired. | Promote. What the waiting does with what it did not ask for is a second question, and a fill for a command the stopping never sent reaches the figures by no other road it controls |
| Row H-200 | A handler that raises is logged and the waiting goes on. | Merge with H-199, which it is the failing of |

| Row H-206 | It calls the stop on the loop reading outcomes last, so the waiting that comes later is meant to be the only reader on that queue. | Merge with H-204: why the order is the order |
| Row H-207 | One still running when the strategies are asked is logged, and the list handed next is built under a lock where one was supplied. | Merge with H-204 on the same terms |

Six rows promote: what a stopping host halts first, what a strategy is handed
when it is asked to stop, the abort a host sends as it stops, what the host
waits for once it has sent, what it does with an outcome it was not waiting
on, and the name an order comes back with. The halting outgrew the cap in the
writing, and stopping the loop that reads outcomes is N6-07; the name outgrew it
as the two roads through the record were made precise, and which name a command
is kept under is N6-08; that in turn outgrew it as the money behind the command
was made precise, and when the money set aside moves is N6-09; the halting outgrew it a second
time as the mode controller was traced, and what the halted mode turns away is
N6-10; it outgrew it a third time as each loop's own stop was traced, and what a
loop's stop leaves running is N6-11.

N6-01 names the watch and goes no further, and N1-02 owns the writing of the mark alone |

N6-11 names the four stops it covers and leaves this one out, and the queued candidate on how a strategy produces the next decision is about the asking rather than the stopping |

Five candidates, three of them Nexus's own and two of them Praxis's. The
stopping sequence is the one that must come first: N6-04 ends by naming
stages that point nowhere.

releasing an order whose own record was lost, what an order still has
against it, whether a fill closes a position, and the sale a host sends as
it stops. The candidates the package-4 re-seed named, taken through the
stages together.

### Merge and promote, the price check

The third subject to outgrow the stage survey, three rounds of findings having
landed on it.

| From | One concrete sentence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Row H-135 | The stage weighs the book's age, its spread, and a price's distance from a reference. | Promote. It is what the stage is |
| Row H-136 | Each runs only where a limit for it is set. | Merge with H-135, which it qualifies |
| Row H-137 | The age is the snapshot's moment less the book's stamp. | Merge with H-135 on the same terms |
| Row H-140 | The deviation's cap is the strategy's where there is one. | Merge with H-135 on the same terms |
| Row H-142 | A deviation past the cap refuses last. | Merge with H-135 on the same terms |
| Row H-138 | A missing stamp refuses, and so does a book stamped later than the snapshot. | Promote. What refuses without weighing anything is a second question |
| Row H-139 | A missing spread figure refuses before the spread is weighed. | Merge with H-138: the same answer for another figure |
| Row H-141 | A deviation refuses on a missing source, a missing figure, or two sources disagreeing. | Merge with H-138 on the same terms |

Two rows promote into one article: what the stage weighs, and what it refuses
without weighing.

## Nexus package 1 — the money and the handover

Scope: the capital a decision reserves and what becomes of it where the decision
never reaches Praxis. Owner: the writer. Pinned at Nexus `953477a`. Review date:
set at the Seed stage below.

**The two rows here were written before this package was seeded.** The brief puts
Seed first — topics proposed, checked as real parts of the domain, and a reviewer
challenging what is missing — then tracing with every behaviour harvested, then
the merge test, and only then writing. These two were traced and reviewed and
corrected, so they are not discarded; but they entered as a pair somebody picked
off a candidate list rather than as the output of a stage, and the Seed below is
run knowing that.

## Seeded for the Nexus side

The brief covers both repositories, and these are the Nexus subjects a reader of
the Praxis side is left needing. Candidates, not approved subjects: each enters a
package through its own Seed stage, and may be rejected or rewritten there. Pinned
at Nexus `953477a`.

| Candidate | What it has to explain |
|---|---|
| What a decision passes through before it is handed over | The ordered checks a decision meets, which actions skip which, and the registration taken before the handover — `nexus/core/validator/pipeline_executor.py`, `nexus/strategy/action_submit.py` |
| What an outcome changes in Nexus | An entry fill against an exit fill, the order the position moves in, what a refusal or a cancellation releases, and why a deposit does not enlarge the pool — `nexus/infrastructure/praxis_connector/outcome_processor.py` |
| What decides whether Nexus may trade at all | Health against the holds, and a daily-loss hold that can clear where a drawdown hold has no clearing branch — `nexus/core/mode_controller.py` |
| How Nexus squares its positions at startup | A position only Nexus has stopping the start, one only Praxis has being considered for import, and a matching one taking Praxis's amount — `nexus/startup/sequencer.py` |
| How a strategy produces the next decision | The loop that asks a strategy, what a prediction is, and how it becomes a request — `nexus/strategy/predict_loop.py`, `nexus/strategy/loader.py` |

## Harvest

Behaviours met while tracing. Each ends as a row, a fold into a row, or an exclusion with a reason. Nothing is dropped silently.

| Harvest ID | Question or observation | Source anchor | From | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H-001 | A newly queued order does not wake the worker. Only an admitted event sets the wake flag; commands wait out the poll interval, currently 0.1s. | `execution_manager.py` `_wait_for_work` 3885-3888, `admit` 3212, `_QUEUE_POLL_INTERVAL` 143 | P1-03 | folds into P1-03, which states the wait, what wakes it, and that queueing wakes nothing |
| H-002 | While the account is still starting, the worker does nothing at all — it sleeps a poll interval and starts the round again. | `execution_manager.py` `_account_loop` 3903-3905 | P1-03 | folds into P1-03, which states the waiting while it starts |
| H-003 | The priority queue drains fully every pass. The command queue yields at most one order per pass. | `execution_manager.py` 3910, 3984 | P1-03 | folds into P1-03, which states one item a pass, and P1-08 the line emptied |
| H-004 | A cancel runs even while the account is reconnecting or stopped; a change is deferred and put back on the queue to retry next pass. | `execution_manager.py` 3912-3928, 3953-3954 | P1-03 | split: cancel half folds P1-04, change half folds P1-08 |
| H-005 | A stopped account fails every waiting order instead of sending it. | `execution_manager.py` `_fail_queued_commands` 3963 | P1-03 | folds into P1-03, which states the queue emptied and each item refused |
| H-006 | While reconnecting but not stopped, the worker halts before the command queue. Waiting orders are neither sent nor ended — they stay. | `execution_manager.py` 3966-3968 | P1-03 | folds into P1-03, which says a paused queue is left as it stands |
| H-007 | Venue news is drained before any new order is started. | `execution_manager.py` `_drain_external_events` 3907 | P1-03 | folds into P1-03, which states venue news drained first |
| H-008 | A sliced order past its deadline is ended without being sent, with no slices placed. A single order has separate deadline handling after submission. | `execution_manager.py` `_expire_stale_command` 4123-4130 | P1-03 | folds into P1-03, which states fed-out work ended instead of started |
| H-009 | Scheme slices, pending protection and a protection sweep all run before the command queue is reached. | `execution_manager.py` 3971-3978 | P1-03 | folds into P1-03, which names all three |
| H-010 | A cancel for an order that has not reached the venue is only marked, not acted on. The mark is what stops it later. | `execution_manager.py` `_process_abort` 8500-8511 | P1-04 | folds into P1-04, which states the marking and the later dropping |
| H-011 | Every starting path reads the cancel mark on entry, four of them as their first statement and the scheme path after working out its schedule, and sets out to end the order with no fills where one is set. Four of the five do end it; the ladder path passes a zero slice total and has its ending refused, as H-042 records. | `execution_manager.py` 4154, 4482, 5275, 5713, 6074 | P1-04 | folds into P1-04, which states the mark read at every start; H-042 carries the ladder |
| H-012 | Because cancels drain fully before any order is taken, a cancel already queued when a pass begins beats the order that pass would have sent. | `execution_manager.py` 3910 with 3984 | P1-04 | folds into P1-08, which states a cancel beating the order a pass would have sent |
| H-013 | A cancel aimed at an order that already finished is dropped at submission and never queued. | `validate_trade_abort.py` 40-53, `execution_manager.py` 3014-3021 | P1-04 | folds into P1-04, which states finished work makes the ask do nothing |
| H-014 | A cancel for an order the venue already has takes a different route: it asks the venue to cancel and records whether the venue confirmed. | `execution_manager.py` `_process_abort` 8515-8532 | P1-04 | folds into P1-04, which states the venue cancel and what it records |
| H-015 | A cancel aimed at a sliced order goes to a separate path rather than the single-order one. | `execution_manager.py` `_abort_scheme` 8489-8491 | P1-04 | folds into P1-04, which states work already begun leaving for its own path |
| H-016 | A price change is not an edit. The resting order is cancelled, the venue is asked what it really filled, and a fresh order is placed for what is left. | `execution_manager.py` `_process_modify` 8716, 8746 into 8779-8782, 8786-8794 and 8797-8799 | P3-13 | folds into P3-25, which states the sequence |
| H-017 | The replacement is placed only after the original is confirmed gone and any fill that raced the cancel has been accounted for. Both orders live at once is not reachable by this route. | `execution_manager.py` `_drive_single_amend` 8776-8800 | P3-13 | folds into P3-25, which states the order of it |
| H-018 | If the racing fill cannot be accounted for, the change is parked and the original is deliberately left unfinished, so the missed fill stays recoverable instead of being stranded. | `execution_manager.py` 8783-8784 with 8747-8748 | P3-13 | folds into P3-25, which states the parking |
| H-019 | The replacement is sized from the command's whole quantity less everything recorded filled across that command's orders, open and closed, the record having first been brought up to the venue's figure for the cancelled order, so a fill the cancel raced is counted rather than ordered a second time. | `execution_manager.py` 8798-8799 summing 2674-2683, brought up to the venue's figure first at 8779-8782 | P3-13 | folds into P3-25, which states the sizing |
| H-020 | A record is written before the cancel, so a restart in the middle rebuilds the sequence rather than reusing an order name. | `execution_manager.py` 8695-8697 into 8700-8713, ahead of the cancel at 8716 | P3-13 | folds into P3-25, which states the record and the restart |
| H-021 | A change aimed at an order that already finished is dropped. A sliced order and a bracket each go to their own change path. | `execution_manager.py` 8615-8624 | P3-13 | folds into P3-13, which states a change to finished work doing nothing |
| H-022 | Two different waits can time out and they are not the same event: the decision side waiting on the execution side, and the execution side waiting on the venue. The reader's evidence and the system's response differ for each. | Nexus `action_submit.py` 337-368; Praxis `execution_manager.py` `_rescue_by_client_order_id` 5513-5542 | timeout (carried) | folds into timeout (carried) |
| H-023 | When the decision side gives up waiting, the order is marked unknown rather than failed. The money stays held and the records stay, so a later report can still be matched to it. | Nexus `action_submit.py` 338-368 | timeout (carried) | folds into timeout (carried) |
| H-024 | When the execution side gives up waiting on the venue, it asks the venue directly whether the order exists, using the name it sent. | Praxis `execution_manager.py` `_rescue_by_client_order_id` 5518-5523 | timeout (carried) | folds into timeout (carried) |
| H-025 | If that question cannot be answered, the order is recorded as refused even though the venue may hold it. The body logs and gives up; that the venue feed later repairs the record appears only in the docstring at 5505-5510 and no body establishes it. | Praxis `execution_manager.py` body 5533-5542 | timeout (carried) | promoted to U-01; repair claim unverified |
| H-026 | The money is given back straight away only for failures that provably happened before the request was handed over. | Nexus `action_submit.py` 273, or 300, or 325 | timeout (carried) | folds into timeout (carried) |
